the body's top-level keys, and the container call (path, key id, which key) when there is one. The fixture
(`test/fixtures/route-snapshot.json`, about 6,000 rows) was written against `main` before the registry existed. **Every
later PR must leave it unchanged**, except that a PR adding a route adds rows (it adds the path to `PATHS`) and changes none
of the existing ones. Regenerate with `npx vitest run test/route-snapshot.test.ts -u` and read the diff.

### 33.2 `defineOp` and the HTTP side

```ts
defineOp({
  name: "library.search",                  // unique
  route?: { method | "ANY", path: "/x/:id" | "/*", params?: { id: /regex/ }, host?: "api" | "share" | "stage" | "studio" },
  auth: "key" | "key_or_service" | "service" | "open" | "capability",
  runs: "worker" | "durable_object",
  cors: boolean,                           // web origin gets access-control-allow-origin on the reply
  noStore?: boolean,                       // cache-control: no-store
  input: zodSchema, output?: zodSchema,
  badRequest?: "error.<area>.bad_request", // the 400 code for a failed input check
  http?: (ctx) => rawInput,                // default: path params + query + JSON body
  ai: { tier: "free" | "ask" | "hidden", summary, call?, aliases?, only?, params?, options?, args?, when?, preview?, result? },
  raw?: boolean,                           // the handler returns a Response (streams)
  handler?: (ctx, input) => { status, body, headers? } | Response,  // throw OpError(status, code, message) to refuse
})
```

- **Order** (`worker.ts`): the share and stage hosts, then the 503 on a bad internal key, the service-credential check
  and the header strip, then **the registry**, then the old gate chain. A request whose method and path no op has (and
  every `OPTIONS`) falls through to the gate unchanged, so a wrong method on a registered path is still the gate's 404.
- **Matching**: whole segments only. A `:param` must match its pattern, and a trailing slash or an extra segment is no
  match.
- **Auth**, the same as the gate plus the Worker's D1 lookup. `key`: an `Api-Key` client key. The library service gets a
  bodyless 404, a missing or bad header gets 401 with the API's own codes, an unknown key gets 401
  `error.api.auth.key.invalid`, and D1 down gets 503. Every one of these carries the web origin's CORS header, as the gate's
  rejections do. `key_or_service`: the service passes as `service:library`. `open`: no key, and `ctx.keyId` is null.
  `capability`: the path is the credential.
- **Input**: a failed zod check is `400 {"status":"error","error":{"code":"<badRequest or error.api.bad_request>"}}`.
  Routes that wrap an older handler keep that handler's own parsing and error codes: they accept any query, and the handler
  reads `ctx.query`.
- **`runs: "durable_object"`** with no handler: the request goes to the main Durable Object with the verified key id
  (`x-cobalt-key-id`) and without the client's `Authorization`, the same way `worker.ts` forwards today.
- **`ctx`** (one per call): `env`, `db`, `now`, `mediaBaseUrl`, `shareBaseUrl`, `edge`, `container`, `registry`, `app()`
  (the `AppDeps` worker.ts builds), `embed()`, `keyId`, `caller` (`key` | `service` | `chat` | `open`), and on HTTP calls
  `request`, `url`, `query` and `params`. It also has `durableObject(path, init)`, which calls the DO with this call's key
  id, and on chat calls `chat` (`threadId`, `collect`, `tools`).
- **Registered so far** (T1): `GET /library` (`library.index`, key or service, CORS), `GET /library/search`
  (`library.query`, key or service, no CORS, no-store), `GET /library/map` (`library.map`, the same), `GET /queue` (`queue`,
  key only, CORS, no-store), and `POST /chat/threads/<id>/confirm/<cid>` (33.5). T5 added every `/share/sets`,
  `/share/collections`, `/share/groups`, `/stage*` owner route and `/emotes` (33.9, 33.10). T7 moved the rest (33.12):
  every route is now an op, and the index at the top of this file lists them.

### 33.3 The chat AI's `cobalt` API

- `registry.toolsFor("chat", { search })` lists the ops whose tier is `free` or `ask`, plus `search` only while the Space
  is configured. The model's cheat sheet (`TOOL_TYPES`, in the system prompt) is generated from them by
  `registry/typescript.ts`. Field comments come from `.describe()`, a schema with `.meta({ id })` prints once as a named type
  (`Post`, `PostFile`), and an `ask` op gets the line "Asks Harmony first".
- **Calls are namespaced by the op's call name**: `cobalt.library.list(opts?)`, `cobalt.library.search(query, limit?)`,
  `cobalt.library.post(id)`, `cobalt.links.list()`, `cobalt.collections.list()`, and, chat-only (`ai.only`),
  `cobalt.look(ids)`, `cobalt.show(ids)` and `cobalt.collage(ids, opts?)`. Results are the same as 27.3 and 27.5.
- **Old names still work** (`ai.aliases`), so saved threads replay: `cobalt.library(opts)`, `cobalt.search(...)`,
  `cobalt.post(id)`, `cobalt.links()` and `cobalt.collections()`. In the sandbox every name is both callable and a namespace,
  and the dotted path is what the Worker's dispatcher looks up.
- **Arguments** map to the input by `ai.params` (positional), then a trailing options object when `ai.options` is set, or
  by `ai.args` (collage keeps its old string title). They are checked with the op's zod schema. A failure throws in the
  model's code as one line: `cobalt.<call>: <field>: <problem>`. A handler's `OpError` throws its message.
- Chat calls run with key id `chat:<thread id>` and `caller: "chat"`. A `durable_object` op called from chat is sent to the
  DO at its route path with the input as JSON.

### 33.4 Ops added in T1

`library.list`, `library.search`, `library.post`, `links.list`, `collections.list`, `look`, `show` and `collage` are the
section 27.3 tools. They are tier `free`, `ai.only`, and call the same functions in `chat-tools.ts`. The four library read
routes are tier `hidden`. Each area adds its ops in `registry/ops/<area>.ts` and lists them in `registry/index.ts`.

### 33.5 Confirm cards (the `ask` tier)

- An `ask` op called from chat **does not run**. Its `ai.preview(ctx, input)` gives `{ summary, effect }` (default: the
  summary's first line and `{}`). The call is stored as one pending card per thread in the main Durable Object's storage
  (`chat:pending:<thread>`, internal paths `/chat/pending/<thread>`, accepted only with key id `chat:<thread>` and never
  routed from the internet) and expires after **15 minutes**. The call returns
  `{ "pending_confirm": { "id": "<16 letters/digits>", "summary", "effect" } }`. Every later call in that run throws
  "waiting for Harmony to answer the confirm card…", later runs in the same reply are refused, and the model is told to say
  in one line what it asked.
- The assistant message gains a step `{ "kind": "confirm", "status": "done", "result": "<summary>", "confirm": <card> }`
  and a top-level `"confirm": null | <card>`, where `<card>` = `{ "id", "op", "summary", "effect", "status":
  "waiting"|"yes"|"no"|"expired", "expires_at": <ms> }`. A waiting card past `expires_at` reads as `expired`. SSE: a new
  event `confirm` `{ "message_id": "<id>", "confirm": <card> }` comes after the step. Older apps decode the step as an
  unknown kind (they show it as a step) and ignore `confirm`.
- **`POST /chat/threads/<id>/confirm/<cid>`** `{ "answer": "yes" | "no" }` (keyed, the service gets 404, CORS, no-store).
  Errors: `400 error.chat.bad_request` (any other answer), `404 error.chat.not_found` (thread),
  `404 error.chat.confirm_gone` (no such card, a different card, expired, or already answered), `409 error.chat.busy|full`,
  `404 error.chat.off`. On success it answers `200 text/event-stream`:
  - first `confirm` `{ "message_id", "confirm": <card with status yes|no> }`, then the usual `assistant`, `text`, `step`,
    `posts`, `collage`, `done` or `error` events (27.2), with **no `user` event**;
  - `yes` runs the stored call once, with the input checked when the model asked and key id `chat:<thread>`. The card's
    step records the result (`status` done or error, `result` the JSON, up to 2,000 chars). `no` runs nothing;
  - the continuation is a new assistant message. The model gets a user turn
    `[Harmony tapped yes on the confirm card "<summary>". cobalt ran it and returned: <json>]` (or `…tapped no… Nothing
    ran.`). It is saved at the start of that message's `api`, so the thread replays.
- T3 adds the first real `ask` ops and the app's card. No migration: cards live in DO storage and in the message's steps.

### 33.6 The sandbox bill (`LOADER.get`)

Dynamic Workers bill one worker per unique id and code per day, and every `load()` counts as a new one. A stable
per-thread id cannot serve per-run code: a cached id keeps its first code, and Workers refuse `eval` and `new Function`, so
the module cannot take the code as an argument. `chat-loader.ts` now calls `LOADER.get("run-<sha256 of the module>", …)`,
so a run whose exact code already ran that day reuses that worker. Anything else is billed as before. It falls back to
`load()` when `get` is missing.

### 33.7 Library writes and the chat's saves (T3)

**Routes moved onto ops** (`registry/ops/library-writes.ts`, all tier `hidden`; nothing on the wire changed and the route
snapshot is identical): `POST /studio` (`studio.create`, still cut to its first link and written to `request_log`),
`PUT /studio/upload` (`studio.upload`), `POST /studio/<sid>/publish` (`studio.publish`), `POST /library/items/<id>/publish`
(`library.publish`), `PATCH /library/items/<id>/visibility`, `PATCH|DELETE /library/items/<id>/post`,
`DELETE /library/items/<id>`, `PUT /library/items/<id>/made`, and the Durable Object's `GET /studio/recent`,
`GET /studio/line`, `DELETE /studio/<sid>/line`, `DELETE /studio/<sid>/render/<job>` and `POST /studio/<sid>/items/retry`
(key only: the library service gets a bodyless 404). Their gate and `worker.ts` branches are gone. `GET /queue` keeps its
route and becomes tier `free` (`cobalt.queue()`).

**The chat's calls** (`ai.only`, key id `chat:<thread id>`). A post id is the `id` the read tools give (the post key):

| call | tier | what it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `cobalt.library.save(url, { items?, public?, title?, wait? })` | free | `POST /studio` to the Durable Object with `queue: true` (never refused as busy), then the save's own long-poll (`GET /studio/<sid>?wait=20`, up to `wait` seconds, default and max 60). Returns `SaveResult { status: ready\|saving\|queued\|error, save_id, post_id, title, kind, files, public, error, saves_left_today }`; `post_id` is set once ready (the session id). |
| `cobalt.library.title(id, title \| null)` | free | `PATCH .../post` on the post's first file (section 15 rules). |
| `cobalt.library.visibility(ids, public)` | ask when `public` is false | Every toggleable file of each post (16.2) that is not already as asked. Public runs at once and returns the new links. |
| `cobalt.library.delete(ids)` | ask | `DELETE .../post` for each post (section 12). A post gone by the time of yes is skipped. |
| `cobalt.library.deleteFile(fileId)` | ask | `DELETE /library/items/<id>` (18.4). A post's original is refused before asking (use `delete`). |
| `cobalt.queue()` | free | `GET /queue`'s body; the line is read with the chat's key id, so `mine: true` are the chat's own saves. |

- `ids` is one post id or a list of 1 to 50, so several posts go on **one** card. An unknown id fails the whole call
  before anything is asked.
- **Per-input ask** (`ai.asks(input)`): an `ask` op with it only asks when it returns true. Making posts public just runs.
  A "make private" with nothing public refuses (`error.library.already_private`) instead of asking.
- **Daily cap**: a thread may save `CHAT_DAILY_SAVES` links (a plain var, default `20`, `0` = off) in any 24 hours,
  counted from `studio_sessions.key_id = chat:<thread>`. Over it the call throws `error.chat.save_cap` with a line telling
  the model to send Harmony to the app. No migration.
- A code run may now take **90 s** of wall time (`CODE_TIMEOUT_MS`, was 30 s) so a save can wait. The CPU limit is unchanged.
- The system prompt gains one paragraph: change the library only when Harmony asks, never save links Harmony did not give,
  and put every post of one delete or "make private" into one call.

**The card's `effect`** for these ops (all keys optional; the app shows what is there):

```json
{ "titles": ["cat jumps", "untitled"], "posts": 2, "files": 3, "bytes": 3000, "public_links": 3, "share_links": 1 }
```

`titles`: up to 5 post titles. `public_links`: public file links that stop working. `share_links`: live gallery share
links (section 24) that show any of these files. `summary` is lowercase: `delete “cat jumps” (3 files)`, `delete 2 posts`,
`make “cat jumps” private`, `delete “clip.mp4” from “three cats”`.

**The app's confirm card** (Apple, `ChatConfirmCard`): shown on the assistant message whose `confirm` is set, or live from
the `confirm` SSE event. It shows the summary, the effect as one line (`3 files · 2.9 kb · 3 public links stop working ·
1 share link`), and while `waiting` two buttons: a red destructive one (`delete N` for `library.delete` /
`library.deleteFile`, N = `effect.files`; `make private` for `library.visibility`; `yes` for any other op) and `cancel`.
A tap calls `POST /chat/threads/<id>/confirm/<cid>` and streams the reply like a send. `yes`, `no` and `expired` show as a
quiet status line instead of the buttons. Older apps show the card's step as a plain step and ignore it.
### 33.8 Make ops (T4): webps, stills, slideshows, gallery images, trims, crops and the editor from chat

Owner: "let the chat AI make things". Ops in `registry/ops/make.ts`; the daily cap in `registry/budget.ts`. **No URL, method,
auth, CORS or body changes**: the route snapshot is unchanged except for the rows of the new internal path below.

**Routes moved onto the registry** (all tier `hidden`; each wraps the old handler with its own parsing and error codes):

| op | route | auth | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `studio.render` | `POST /studio/<sid>/render` (4, 10, 18.13) | `capability` | the request goes to the Durable Object exactly as it came (no key id) |
| `studio.render_status` | `GET /studio/<sid>/render/<job>` (4) | `capability` | the same; `DELETE` (17.7 cancel) stays with the gate |
| `studio.slideshow` | `POST /studio/<sid>/slideshow` (18.5, 18.10, 18.14) | `key` | Durable Object with the verified key id |
| `studio.gallery_image` | `POST /studio/<sid>/gallery-image` (18.11) | `key` | the same |
| `library.reopen` | `POST /library/items/<id>/studio` (5d, 17.3, 18.14) | `key_or_service` | CORS; `?queue` as before (`400 error.library.bad_request`) |
| `edits.route.*` | the ten `/edits` routes (31.12) | `key` | no CORS, no-store, `404 error.edit.off` without the renderer binding |

Their branches are gone from `gate.ts` (`decideEdits`, the render, slideshow, gallery image and reopen branches) and from
`worker.ts`. Every other method or path under them is still the gate's 404. `/studio*` answers still get the studio CORS
wrap in `handleRequest`.

**The chat AI's calls** (tier `free` unless noted, `ai.only`, key id `chat:<thread id>`). Every `id` is a post id or one
file's id from `library.post`; a post id means its first source file of the right type (an item or the original, never a
made file):

- `cobalt.make.webp(id, { start, end, crop?, width?: 320|480, quality? })`: the 4/10/18.13 render of 0.5 to 10 s of a video
  or gif. It runs on the file's own session while that is open (a gallery item with `item: <index>`), else the file is
  reopened as the chat (`library.reopen`'s handler with `queue`) and rendered once the session is ready.
- `cobalt.make.still(id, { seconds?, width?: 320|480|640, quality? })`: the 18.14 webp of one photo (jpeg, png, webp).
- `cobalt.make.slideshow(id, { items?: fileIds, seconds?, format?, fade?, frame?, sound?, width?, quality? })` and
  `cobalt.make.galleryImage(id, { layout?, items? })`: 18.5/18.10/18.11 on the gallery's own session, so only within its 7
  days. `seconds` is per photo; videos and gifs get `null`.
- `cobalt.make.trim(id, { start, end, fit?, canvas?, title? })` and `cobalt.make.crop(id, { x, y, w, h }, { start?, end?, … })`:
  a one-clip editor project (31.2; canvas from the picture's shape as 31.2.1, 1080p 30 fps, `fit` default `"fit"`) rendered at
  once to an mp4. A project whose render is refused is deleted again. Answers `Job & { project_id }`.
- `cobalt.edits.list()`, `.get(id)`, `.create(timeline, { title? })`, `.update(id, timeline, { title? })` (sends the current
  revision as `base_revision`), `.render(id)`, and `.delete(id)` (tier `ask`, card "delete the edit "<title>"", effect
  `{ edits: 1, clips }`). The timeline input is checked by the editor's own `parseTimeline` (31.3) wrapped in zod; a refusal
  reads `cobalt.edits.create: timeline: <code> at <path>`. The model's cheat sheet gets the timeline's shape as a comment.
- `cobalt.jobs.wait(job, seconds ≤ 25, default 15)`: the plan said 60, but a code run stops at 30 s (`CODE_TIMEOUT_MS`), so the model
  waits again in the next run. Job ids: `studio:<sid>:<job>` (polled through `GET /studio/<sid>/render/<job>?wait=≤10` in
  the Durable Object), `edit:<rid>` (D1, every 3 s), and `open:<sid>:<plan>` (a reopened file still opening: the wait
  advances the save and starts the make when it is ready, then returns the new `studio:` id). Answer, the `Job` type:
  `{ job, status: "opening"|"queued"|"running"|"done"|"error"|"cancelled", progress?, queue_ahead?, error?, file_id?,
  post_id?, kind?, bytes?, width?, height?, seconds?, public?, share_url? }`. Done answers name the new file and its post;
  `share_url` only when it is public (never a raw media URL).

**Keyed studio routes run as the session's owner.** The Durable Object lets only a session's creating key add to it
(slideshow, gallery image). The chat is Harmony's own, so the chat's slideshow, still and gallery image calls carry the
session's `key_id` (a reopened file's session is the chat's own). The render route has no key, as before.

**Daily cap.** Every start (each `make.*`, `edits.render`, and the start inside `jobs.wait` for an `open:` job) takes one
unit of a per-thread, per-UTC-day counter, `CHAT_DAILY_RENDERS` (a plain var, default `10`, `0..1000`). At the cap the call
throws `cobalt.<call>: this chat already made <n> files today, …` and nothing starts (a trim makes no project). A start
that is refused, or an `edits.render` that answers an already running render, gives its unit back. The counter lives in
the main Durable Object's storage (`chat:budget:<thread>:<kind>` = `{ day, n }`), reached through the internal
`POST /chat/budget/<thread>/<kind>` `{ "limit": n }` or `{ "refund": true }`, accepted only with key id `chat:<thread>`;
from the internet it is the gate's 404 (snapshot rows added). No migration.

**System prompt.** While `make` calls are in the cheat sheet, the prompt says to make things only when Harmony asks, that
editor mp4s cost real money, to wait with `jobs.wait`, and to `show()` the result's post and give its `share_url`.

**Not moved here**: `POST /webp` and `GET /webp/<id>` (the older link webp service with the request log) stay with the gate
for T7. `DELETE /studio/<sid>/render/<job>` (cancel) is T3's.
### 33.9 Share ops (T5): gallery links, collections and sets

`registry/ops/share.ts`. **The routes** of sections 24, 26 and 28 are tier `hidden` ops that call the same handlers with the
same parsing, codes and headers: `GET|POST /share/sets`, `DELETE /share/sets/<19 digits>`, `GET|POST /share/collections`,
`PATCH|DELETE /share/collections/<id>`, `POST /share/collections/<id>/reroll`, `GET|POST /share/groups` and
`PATCH|DELETE /share/groups/<id>` (keyed, the service gets 404, CORS, `cache-control: no-store`; the share host off is still
`404 error.share.off` without no-store). The route snapshot is unchanged.

**The chat AI's calls** (`ai.only`, key id `chat:<thread>`, so rows it makes carry that `key_id`). A collection or a set is
named by its id or its name (case-insensitive; the most recently changed wins a tie). A link is its 19-digit id or its
`<share>/g/<id>` URL. A refusal throws one line that says what to do.

- `cobalt.links.create(ids, { title? })` → `{ id, url, title, files }` (free). `ids` are post ids, or file ids from
  `library.post`. Each post brings its public files: a gallery post all its public items in order, any other post its
  lead file when public, else its newest public file. 2 to 10 files make a gallery link (`POST /share/sets`, so the
  caption is written as in section 25). One file answers that file's own share page `<share>/<name>` with `id: null`.
  The URL is always on the share host, never a media URL. A private post, an unknown id or more than 10 files is refused
  before anything is made.
- `cobalt.links.revoke(link)` (**ask**; effect `{ link, files, collections }`).
- `cobalt.collections.create(name, { links? })`, `cobalt.collections.add(collection, links)` (makes the collection when
  no collection has that name; the result has `created`), `cobalt.collections.update(collection, { name?, add?, remove? })`
  (free); `cobalt.collections.reroll(collection)` (**ask**: every old URL stops working; effect `{ collection, links,
  old_links_stop_working }`) and `cobalt.collections.delete(collection)` (**ask**; effect `{ collection, links,
  links_stay_live }`). Results are `{ id, name, link_ids }`, the shape of `collections.list`.
- `cobalt.sets.list()`, `cobalt.sets.create(name, { collections? })`, `cobalt.sets.add(set, collections)` (makes the set
  when missing), `cobalt.sets.update(set, { name?, add?, remove? })` (free) and `cobalt.sets.delete(set)` (**ask**; effect
  `{ set, collections, collections_stay }`). Results are `{ id, name, collection_ids }`.

### 33.10 Stage and emote ops (T5)

`registry/ops/stage.ts`. **The routes** of section 30.10 (`GET|PATCH /stage`, `POST /stage/rotate`, `POST /stage/action`,
`GET /stage/pool`, `GET /stage/redemptions`, `PATCH /stage/rewards/<summon|chaos|shuffle|pin>`, `POST /stage/twitch/connect`,
`DELETE /stage/twitch`) and section 20 (`GET|POST /emotes`, keyed or the service, CORS) are tier `hidden` ops around the
same handlers. Rewards, redemptions, rotating the canvas token and Twitch stay owner-only: the AI has no call for them.

The chat AI's calls (the app calls the stage "studio"; the model is told so):

- `cobalt.stage.get()` → `<stage>` (30.10); `cobalt.stage.set({ on_air?, density?, tile_seconds?, tile_scale?,
  spotlight_auto?, spotlight_seconds?, sound?, sound_all?, play_through?, video_max?, store_hint? })` → `<stage>` (free;
  `PATCH /stage`, clamped as there; any other key, `store_open` included, is refused).
- `cobalt.stage.store(open)` (**ask**: lock or unlock the viewer store; effect `{ store_url, now, after }`).
- `cobalt.stage.pool(pool)` → `{ pool, links?, not_public? }` (free). `pool` is `"library"`, `{ collection }`, `{ set }`
  (an id or a name), `{ search: "words" }`, or **an array of post ids**. Picks need no new pool kind (and no migration):
  their public files (at most 200, at least 2) become gallery links of 2 to 10 files titled "stage picks", without inline
  captions (the 15-minute cron writes them), filed in the collection "stage picks" (made when missing), and the pool
  becomes that collection. The collection then holds only this call's links; links from earlier picks stay live in the
  links tab. Private picks are left out and named in `not_public`.
- `cobalt.stage.items({ offset?, limit? })` (`GET /stage/pool`), `cobalt.stage.action(type, { item_id? })`
  (`POST /stage/action`), free.
- `cobalt.emotes.find({ query?, sort?, page? })` (`GET /emotes`, section 20.1; a query, a sort or both; `sort` defaults to
  `top`) → `{ sort, page, has_more, items }`, and `cobalt.emotes.save(id)` (`POST /emotes`), free. Both are MCP tools too.

### 33.11 The workbench box: `cobalt.box.run` and `cobalt.box.save` (T6)

A Linux box (yt-dlp, curl, ffmpeg, python3) the chat AI uses as a **last resort**, for a site or a file the normal tools
cannot save. **No new route, no migration, no secret, nothing for the app.** The ops are `ai.only`, tier `free`, and the
model sees them only while the box is on (`WORKBENCH` bound and `WORKBENCH_DAILY_MINUTES` above 0).

- **Where it runs**: the `Workbench` Durable Object (`src/workbench.ts`, one object, `"main"`) on its own container app
  `cobalt-api-workbench` (image `deploy/cloudflare/workbench`, `standard-1` since 33.11.1: 1/2 vCPU, 4 GiB, 8 GB disk, `maxInstances: 1`). It is a
  Containers-library `Container` with no port: commands go through the container exec API. The decisions are in
  `src/workbench-core.ts`, the rules in `src/workbench-rules.ts` (both node-testable). It does **not** use
  `@cloudflare/sandbox`: since 1.0 that package is only file helpers on top of the same exec API, and it would need
  `nodejs_compat` on the whole API Worker.
- **One box, one thread at a time**. A call from another thread **wipes** the box (destroy, then a fresh start) before it
  runs, so no file crosses threads. It sleeps **2 minutes** after its last call (`sleepAfter`; a running command keeps it
  awake), and a sleeping container keeps nothing.
- **No secrets**: no env vars at all. Commands run as the unprivileged user `box` (uid 1000) in `/workspace`; only
  `box-init` (adds the platform's interception CA to the system bundle) runs as root, once per start.
- **Outbound** (`checkOutbound`): `enableInternet: false`, and every HTTP and HTTPS request (`interceptHttps`) goes to the
  library's catch-all outbound handler in the Worker (`ContainerProxy`, exported from `src/index.ts`). It applies the 19.2
  rule: http/https only, no `user:pass@`, port 80 or 443, an IP literal must be public (the same tables as `isPublicIp`), a
  name must have a dot and not be a private spelling. On top of it, **our own zone is refused** (`*.capybaraharmony.com`,
  except the public `media.` host) and so are `*.workers.dev` and `*.pages.dev`. A refusal is `403` with the body
  `workbench: blocked (<reason>)`. Allowed requests are fetched by the Worker with `redirect: "manual"`, so each hop comes
  back through the rule. Names resolve on Cloudflare's network, which cannot reach private addresses. Other ports and raw
  TCP have no route out, and HTTPS to a bare IP fails (interception needs a name).
- **`cobalt.box.run(cmd, { timeoutS? })`**: `bash -c cmd`, default 60 s, at most 120 s (then killed, `code: 124`,
  `timed_out: true`). Returns `{ code, stdout, stderr, timed_out }`. Each stream is cut to 8 KB: the first 2 KB and the last
  6 KB, with `… (N bytes cut) …` between.
- **`cobalt.box.save(path, { title?, public? })`**: the file's size and first 64 bytes are read, the type comes **from the
  bytes** (jpg, png, webp, gif, heic, mp4/m4v by a video `ftyp` brand, mov), and the bytes stream into **the
  `PUT /studio/upload` code** (`studioUpload`) with key id `chat:<thread>`, `queue=1`, the file name with the sniffed
  extension, and `title`/`public` when given. So it lands in the library like any upload: a private `upload` row, R2
  `uploads/<id>.<ext>`, and a studio session for a video. Its cap is that path's **100 MB** (`MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`), not the
  200 MB direct-link cap. Returns `{ post_id, kind, bytes, processing, public_url }`. A webm, audio, html or anything else is
  refused with a hint to convert it with ffmpeg.
- **Daily cap**: `WORKBENCH_DAILY_MINUTES` (default 60) awake minutes per UTC day across all threads, counted in the
  object's storage (`workbench:ledger`). Awake time runs from start to stop. A stop the object learns about late is charged
  up to the last call plus 2 minutes. Past the cap the box is put to sleep and every call throws "the workbench is used up
  for today".
- **Run budget**: a code run whose code mentions `box` gets 240 s instead of 30 s (`CODE_TIMEOUT_BOX_MS`), and the message
  is saved every 30 s meanwhile so it never reads as stale (27.2, `STALE_MS`).
- **The system prompt** gains a paragraph only while the box is offered: try the normal calls first, keep commands quiet,
  finish with `cobalt.box.save`, never send anything from the library to a site.
- **Cost** (Workers Paid rates): memory 1 GiB × 3,600 s × $0.0000025 = **$0.009**, disk 4 GB × 3,600 s × $0.00000007 =
  **$0.001**, CPU on active use only, up to 1/4 vCPU × 3,600 s × $0.00002 = **$0.018**. That is **$0.01 to $0.03 per awake
  hour**, before the monthly allowances, plus egress at $0.025/GB past 1 TB (North America, Europe). The 60-minute cap bounds it
  at about $0.85 a month.
- **Deploy** (owner): an API deploy builds and pushes the new image and creates the container app and the Durable Object
  class. No migration, no secret.

### 33.11.1 FFmpeg editing on the box: `cobalt.box.pull`

The chat AI and the MCP server (35) get every ffmpeg operation for editing, not only the fixed make/edits ops, by running
ffmpeg on the workbench box against library files. **Nothing for the app, no route, no migration, no secret.** The same
three ops are the tools on both sides (`box_run`, `box_pull`, `box_save` over MCP).

- **`cobalt.box.pull(id, { as? })`**: `id` is a post id (the files it was saved as, every item of a gallery, up to 10;
  a post with none of those gives all its files) or one file id (any file, made ones too). Each file streams from R2
  (`cobalt-originals` or `cobalt-media` by the row's bucket) into `/workspace/<file id>.<ext>` (ext from the content type,
  else the key), or `/workspace/<as>` for a single file (`as` matches `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,127}$`). Written as
  `<name>.part` and renamed; a size that differs from R2's is an error. At most **1 GB** a call. Returns
  `{ files: [{ path, file_id, content_type, bytes, seconds, width, height }] }`. Private files are allowed.
- **Outbound body cap**: because private bytes can now be in the box, a request from the box with a body over **64 KB**
  (declared or streamed) is refused with `workbench: blocked (a request body over 64 KB)`. Sites' own API calls (forms,
  GraphQL) still fit. The only way a file leaves is `box.save`, into the library.
- **Flow**: `box.pull` -> `box.run("ffmpeg -hide_banner -loglevel error -y -i <in> ... out.mp4")` -> `box.save("out.mp4")`.
  `box.save`'s types stay jpg, png, webp, gif, heic, mp4, mov (100 MB).
- **Size**: the box moved from `basic` to `standard-1` for ffmpeg (about $0.08 an awake hour; the 60-minute daily cap
  bounds it at about $2.50 a month). The 120 s per command and 240 s per code run limits are unchanged, so the prompt asks
  for short outputs, scaled-down inputs and `-preset veryfast`.
- **Prompt**: the workbench paragraph now names both uses (editing beyond the make/edits calls; last resort for saving).

### 33.12 Every route is an op (T7)

T7 moved the routes still on the old chain onto ops and deleted the chain. **Nothing on the wire changed**: the route
snapshot (33.1) is byte for byte the same, plus the rows of one new sample path (below). The route index at the top of this
file is generated from the registry (33.13).

- **`worker.ts`** now only picks the host, refuses a bad internal key (503), reads the service credential, strips the
  internal headers, and hands the request to the registry. **`gate.ts`** keeps the `Authorization` check, the id patterns,
  and one rule for what no op takes: an `OPTIONS` from the web origin is answered (204 by the Worker under `/studio*`, by
  the container elsewhere), an `OPTIONS` from any other origin is 403, an `OPTIONS` on `/live*` or `/telemetry` is 404,
  and everything else is a bodyless 404.
- **New op files** in `src/registry/ops/`: `system.ts` (`GET /capabilities`, `POST /telemetry`, `POST /` and `GET /`,
  `GET /tunnel`, `POST /webp`, `GET /webp/<id>`, `DELETE /media/<name>`), `live.ts` (the Live Activity routes of sections
  8, 27.9 and 32, and the Hark opt-ins of 9 and 17.6), `library-admin.ts` (`GET /studio/<sid>`, `GET|HEAD
  /studio/<sid>/source`, `GET|HEAD /library/items/<id>/file`, `POST /library/adopt`, `GET /library/search/post`, `POST
  /library/search/index`, `POST /library/posters/backfill`, `POST /library/visibility/migrate`) and `hosts.ts`. The chat
  thread routes (27.2) joined `chat.ts`. All are tier `hidden`.
- **The other hosts are ops** too: the share host's link pages (`host.share`), the stage host (`host.stage`) and any other
  `studio.*` host's 404 page (`host.studio`). Each is one raw catch-all (`route: { host, method: "ANY", path: "/*" }`). They
  still run before the internal-key check and see the request unstripped, as before.
- **`auth: "service"`** is new: the library service only; anyone else gets `401 error.api.auth.key.invalid` (with the web
  origin's CORS), as the gate answered `POST /library/adopt`. An `open` op sees `ctx.caller === "service"` for the service
  (`GET /capabilities` reports it).
- **`raw: true`** marks every op whose handler answers with its own `Response`: uploads, file bytes, the chat stream, the
  studio status and source, the share and stage pages, and the forwarding of `POST /`, `GET /`, `GET /tunnel`, the webp
  routes and adopt.
- **A fix**: `POST /chat/threads/<id>/confirm/<cid>` accepted any thread id. Its pattern came from `chat.ts`, which imports
  the registry, and through that cycle it was still undefined when the op was defined, so the segment matched anything.
  The id patterns now come from `gate.ts` (no imports), and `defineOp` refuses a `params` entry that is not a `RegExp`. The
  snapshot gained the path `/chat/threads/bad/confirm/<id>`: every method and caller is the 404 (or the preflight answer)
  it should be.

### 33.13 Generated files: `npm run gen`

`npm run gen` in `deploy/cloudflare/api` (`scripts/run-gen.mjs` bundles `scripts/gen.ts` with esbuild and runs it) writes,
from the registry:

- **`deploy/cloudflare/api/openapi.json`** (OpenAPI 3.1): every API-host route with its `operationId` (the op name), path
  parameters and their patterns, the query or JSON body when the op's input schema names them, the reply schema when the op
  has an `output`, and `security` (`apiKey`: `Authorization: Api-Key <uuid>`; `service`: `x-cobalt-service`). Each operation
  carries `x-cobalt` `{ auth, runs, cors, no_store?, raw?, input?, ai }`; `input: "read by the handler"` means the op wraps
  an older handler that parses the request itself (its section here is the reference). The share, stage and studio hosts
  are not in it.
- **The route index** at the top of this file, between the `routes:begin` and `routes:end` markers, and the list of the
  chat AI's calls.
- **`apple/CobaltKit/Sources/CobaltKit/API/Generated/Endpoints.swift`**: `Endpoints.<op name in camel case>(<path ids>)`
  returns an `APIEndpoint<Response>` (method, path with the ids escaped, auth). `Response` is the generated reply type when
  the op has an `output`, else `APIUntyped`. Reply structs follow the JSON keys through `convertFromSnakeCase`. A shape with
  `x-swift` in its meta reuses that CobaltKit type, and a list of one is decoded row by row (`Lossy`). Everything is
  internal to CobaltKit.
- **Reply shapes** live in `src/registry/shapes.ts` and describe what the handlers already answer; they never build or
  rewrite a reply. So far: `GET /library` (`LibraryIndexShape`; a post is a loose object, since its fields grow with `v`)
  and `GET /share/collections` (`ShareCollectionsListShape`). `test/gen.test.ts` checks real answers against them.
- **Staleness**: `npm run gen:check` writes nothing and fails when an output differs; `test/gen.test.ts` fails the same way
  in `npm test`. A GitHub workflow that runs the check, the typecheck and the snapshot on every PR touching
  `deploy/cloudflare/` is ready to add as `.github/workflows/cobalt-worker.yml` (the session that wrote it had no permission
  to push workflow files; the owner adds it).
- **The app**: `HTTPCobaltClient.library(...)` and `.shareCollections()` build their requests from `Endpoints.libraryIndex()`
  and `Endpoints.shareCollectionsList()` and decode the generated reply types (`HTTPCobaltClient+Endpoints.swift`). The
  library screens and the collection screens (links tab, collection picker) get their data through these two calls; their
  behaviour is unchanged. The other calls move over screen by screen.

### 33.14 Job hooks: `cobalt.jobs.onDone` (addendum, 2026-10-28; owner: "I dont like how it polled it should have a hook from the server as a primitive")

Before this, the chat AI waited for a job it started with `cobalt.jobs.wait` (at most 25 s per call), so a webp render
took several code runs that each answered `running`, and the reply often gave up with "say the word and I'll check".
Now the server watches the job and continues the chat itself.

- **The call** (chat only, tier free, no route): `cobalt.jobs.onDone(job: string, note?: string)`. It first waits up to
  8 s (`JOB_ONDONE_FIRST_WAIT_S`); a job that ended by then answers its result at once, like `jobs.wait`. Otherwise it hands
  the job (its newest id) to the thread's watcher and answers the job's state with `watching: true`. `note` (at most 300
  characters) is what the model means to do with the result ("show it and give the share link"); it comes back with the
  result. Without the `CHAT_JOBS` binding it only looks (no `watching`).
- **The reply ends**: the run that handed a job over gets a `note` telling the model the server is watching, and any later
  `run_code` in that reply is refused ("the server continues this chat when the job ends"). The message gets one step per
  job: `{ kind: "job", status: "running", job: { job, note, status: "waiting" } }` (no code).
- **The watcher**: the `ChatJobs` Durable Object (`src/chat-jobs-do.ts`, rules in `src/chat-jobs.ts`), one instance per
  thread (`idFromName(<thread id>)`), reached only from the Worker (`PUT /watch`, `x-internal-key`). Its alarm looks at each
  job every 4 s with the same code as `jobs.wait` (wait 0), as the thread's chat key (`chat:<thread id>`): a studio job
  through the main Durable Object's status route, an editor render in D1, a file still opening is started when it is
  ready (its id becomes `studio:...`). At most 5 jobs per thread. A job ends when it is `done`, `error` or `cancelled`; one
  still going after an hour ends as `timeout`, and one whose state cannot be read 15 ticks in a row as `error`
  (`error.chat.job_unreadable`).
- **The continuation**: when jobs end, the alarm marks their `job` steps (`status` `done` or `error`, `job.status` the
  outcome, `result` the job's JSON) and writes a new assistant message, exactly like the reply after a confirm card
  (33.5): the model's opening user turn is a note from the server with each job's result and note, and it carries on
  (`cobalt.show`, the share link, or a one-line failure). It is saved to D1 as it goes; there is no stream. Its Live
  Activity (27.9) goes to every device with a chat start token (the sender is `server:chat-jobs`, so none is left out).
  A thread with a reply still running is tried again on each tick for up to 10 minutes; a deleted or full thread drops
  its jobs.
- **The model's instructions**: "Making files" in the system prompt now says to pass every job to `jobs.onDone` in the
  same run, end the reply in one line when it answers `watching: true`, and never poll `jobs.wait` or ask Harmony to say
  when to check. `jobs.wait` stays for a quick look.
- **The app**: `ChatStep.job` (`ChatJobCard`) and `isWaitingJob`; `ChatThreadContent.isWaitingOnJob`. While the open
  thread waits on a job, `ChatStore` reads it every 4 s (at most an hour) until the server's reply appears, then follows
  that reply like any other. The step row says "making it… the chat carries on when it's ready", then "made it" or
  why it did not finish.
- **Deploy**: a new Durable Object class and binding (`CHAT_JOBS` → `ChatJobs`, sqlite) in `cloudflare.config.ts`. No
  secret, no D1 migration, no route change (the route snapshot is unchanged).

## 34. Search inside collections, "what it's about", and sound (addendum, pinned 2026-10-30; owner: "for the ai to see the "furry" videos in a collection", "we have embeddings but I cannot see what words those embeddings are bound to", "can we include sound in a final plan", "implement everything"; plan `collection-search/PLAN.md`, mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/1ScE9CALDGmK4kuQqJg8Xn)

Before this, a post's vector (section 21) came from its title (custom title, else the file name), its file names and link words,
and its poster. For most saved reels that is a file name like `instagram_De84mWBOe72` plus one frame. The chat's `search` covered
the whole library (top N) and could not be limited to a collection, and `collections()` returned link ids, not posts. This section:
limits search to a collection, a link or a list of posts; describes every post in plain words from three frames and its sound; puts
those words into the vector; and shows them, with the words the vector reads closest to, on the post's detail.

### 34.1 D1: migration `0022_about.sql` (additive; the owner applies it before the API deploy)

- `media_about`: one row per post (the `GET /library` post key).
  `post_key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, item_id TEXT NOT NULL` (the lead file, as `postInputs` picks it),
  `probe_state TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending'` (`pending` | `done` | `none` (nothing to cut: no video file, or an image) | `failed`),
  `probe_at INTEGER` (ms, last probe attempt), `probe_attempts INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`,
  `frames INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (frame pictures stored, 0..3), `has_audio INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`,
  `transcript TEXT` (at most 4,000 characters, null when no speech or no sound), `language TEXT`,
  `sentence TEXT` (at most 160 characters), `tags TEXT` (JSON array, what Haiku wrote), `sound_tags TEXT` (JSON array),
  `user_tags TEXT` (JSON array; when not null it replaces `tags` everywhere: the owner's edits win and survive a rewrite),
  `model TEXT`, `input_hash TEXT` (what the words were written from), `written_at INTEGER`, `failed_at INTEGER`,
  `attempts INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, `updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL`.
- `embed_terms`: the word list for "reads closest to". `term TEXT PRIMARY KEY, source TEXT NOT NULL` (`starter` | `tag`),
  `model TEXT, dims INTEGER, vector TEXT` (base64 float32 like `media_embeddings.vector`, null until embedded),
  `mean REAL` (the term's average cosine over every indexed post, refreshed by the cron), `created_at INTEGER NOT NULL`.
- `chat_messages.ranked TEXT` (nullable JSON, 34.6).

### 34.2 Frames and sound: the probe (container helper, one ffmpeg at a time)

- A new job kind in the main Durable Object's sweep, `about:<item id>`, run exactly like a poster job (section 13): queued by
  `POST /about/kick?limit=N` (internal, from the 15-minute cron, at most 25 per kick, lead files of posts whose `probe_state` is
  `pending`, or `failed` with `probe_at` older than a day and fewer than 3 attempts), one job per pass, only while the helper is free,
  and a save or render arriving meanwhile waits for it.
- Helper `POST /about?id=<item id>` (port 9100, `x-internal-key`; named `/about` because the helper's `/probe` already measures
  stored uploads): the stored video in the body (the same streaming as `/poster`), answers JSON
  `{ "frames": ["<base64 jpeg>", ...], "audio": "<base64>"|null, "duration": <s>|null }`: 3 frames at 15 %, 50 % and 85 % of the
  duration (one frame for a clip under 2 s), each scaled so the long side is at most 768 px; `audio` is the first 180 s as mono
  16 kHz MP3 at 32 kbit/s, or null when the file has no audio stream or it is silent (mean volume under -60 dB). 90 s wall.
  Errors: `400 error.studio.not_video` (the row becomes `none`), `429` busy (not counted as an attempt), `422 error.about.failed`
  (ffmpeg failed or ran past the wall); anything but 429 and 400 counts as a failed attempt.
- The Durable Object stores them in R2 `cobalt-originals` under `about/<item id>/frame-1.jpg` .. `frame-3.jpg` and
  `about/<item id>/audio.mp3` (private; deleted with the item) and writes `probe_state`, `frames`, `has_audio`, `probe_at`.
- Images skip the probe: `probe_state = 'none'` and the writer looks at the post's poster or the image itself (public, else read
  from R2 when under 5 MB). A gallery uses its lead file.

### 34.3 The words: transcript (Whisper) and description (Haiku), from the 15-minute cron

- Runs after the roast captions and before the search indexer, up to 120 s a run (`ABOUT_RUN_BUDGET_MS`), newest posts first.
  A post is due when its probe is `done` or `none` and its `input_hash` differs from the current input (frames, transcript, title,
  link), or it failed more than a day ago with fewer than 3 attempts. A post whose probe is `pending` or `failed` is not described
  (one that fails the probe 3 times is never described). The search indexer's drain gets what is left of its budget after this run
  and the word list (34.5).
- Sound: with the `AI` binding (Workers AI, new in `cloudflare.config.ts`) and `has_audio`, `@cf/openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo`
  transcribes `audio.mp3`; the text (trimmed to 4,000) and detected language are stored. No speech: transcript null. Whisper off or
  failing: the description is written without it.
- Description: Claude Haiku 5.5 (`ABOUT_MODEL`, default `claude-haiku-5-5`) through `ModelRelay` (like every Anthropic call), with
  the frames (base64 images), the title, the link's site and the transcript. Plain, factual wording (not the caption voice: these
  words are for search). Structured output `{ "sentence": "<=160 chars", "tags": ["5..12 lowercase words or short phrases, <=30 chars"],
  "sound_tags": ["0..6, e.g. music, laughter, screaming, phonk, asmr; [] when silent"] }`. No guesses about who people are.
- Off unless `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set. Cost (owner asked): about $0.0006 a post for Haiku and under $0.001 for Whisper.

### 34.4 What a post is embedded from (changes section 21)

`text` becomes, joined with ` · ` and cut at 3,000 characters: the file names, the link words, the sentence, the tags (`user_tags`
when set), the sound tags, the caption title (section 25, when the post has one), and the first 1,200 characters of the transcript.
`title` is unchanged. A changed input changes `input_hash`, so every post is embedded once more after the words arrive (the cron
drains it; `POST /library/search/index?drain=1` speeds it up). `GET /library/search/post`'s `input.text` shows the new text.

### 34.5 The word list and "reads closest to"

- `src/about-terms.ts` holds a starter list of about 800 everyday words and short phrases (animals, fandoms, games, places,
  moods, sounds, kinds of video: "fursuit", "furry", "cosplay", "cat", "airplane", "phonk", "laughter", "jump scare", ...).
  Every tag and sound tag any post has is added with `source = 'tag'`.
- The cron embeds terms that have no vector, in query mode (`"query"`, the same call the search makes), 32 per Space call, inside
  the indexer's budget, and refreshes `mean` for every term after a run that indexed anything.
- A post's nearest words: for each embedded term, `cosine(post, term) - mean`, best 8 with that value rounded to 2 places.
  Computed when asked (800 x 256 multiplications).

### 34.6 Routes and ops (section 33; `npm run gen` after)

- **`library.search`** (`GET /library/search`, `cobalt.library.search`): new optional `within`. On the route: `collection=<id or
  name>`, `link=<set id>`, or `posts=<id,id,...>` (at most 500); exactly one (more than one, a collection over 200 characters or a link id over 64:
  `400 error.library.bad_request`). In chat: `search(query, { within: { collection }
  | { link } | { posts }, limit? })` (the old `search(query, limit)` still works; a string `within` means a collection id or name).
  With `within`, every post in it is scored (no top-N cut; `limit` default and maximum 500), posts without a vector get their text
  score only, every hit carries `"indexed": <bool>` (only when `within` is given), and the answer adds `"within": { "kind": "collection"|"link"|"posts", "id": <id>|null,
  "name": <name>|null, "posts": <n> }`. Every hit (with or without `within`) gains `"why": ["<up to 3 of the post's tags or sound
  tags, closest to the query first>"]` (empty when the post has no words yet). Unknown collection or link: `404
  error.collection.not_found` / `404 error.share.not_found`. A collection's posts are the posts of its live links' files.
- **`collections.list`** in chat (`cobalt.collections()`, `cobalt.collections.list`): each collection also carries `post_ids`.
  The route's body is unchanged.
- **`library.about`** (new; `GET /library/about?key=<post key>`, keyed or service; chat `cobalt.library.about(key)`, tier free; the chat's answer leaves out `status` and `posts`) →
  `200 { "status": "success", "post_key", "probe": "pending"|"done"|"none"|"failed",
  "about": null | { "sentence" (null while only the owner's tags exist), "tags": [...], "edited": <bool, user_tags set>, "sound_tags": [...], "transcript": <string|null>,
  "language": <string|null>, "frames": <0..3>, "has_audio": <bool>, "model", "written_at": <ms>|null } (null until Haiku wrote it or the owner set tags),
  "words": [ { "word": "fursuit", "score": 0.42 }, ... ] (empty without a vector or terms),
  "neighbours": [ { "post_id", "score" } ] (the 4 nearest posts by vector),
  "posts": { "<post id>": <GET /library?v=3 post shape> } (the neighbours),
  "index": <the body of GET /library/search/post minus "status"> }`. Unknown key: `404 error.library.not_found`. Before the
  migration: `about: null`, `probe: "pending"`, empty `words`. Search off: `words` and `neighbours` empty.
- **`library.about.set`** (new; `PATCH /library/about` `{ "key": "<post key>", "tags": ["..."] | null }`, keyed only; chat `cobalt.library.about.set(key, tags)`, tier
  ask): replaces the owner's tags (0..16, each trimmed, lowercased, 1..30 code points, duplicates dropped); `null` goes back to
  Haiku's. Answers the `GET /library/about` body. The post's input changes, so it is embedded again on the next pass. Bad body:
  `400 error.library.bad_request`.
- **`chat.rank`** (chat only, tier free, no route): `cobalt.rank(hits, { title?, query?, within? })` attaches a scored list to the
  reply: `hits` is `[{ id, score?, why? }]` (at most 50, unknown ids dropped, `why` at most 4 strings of 30), `title` at most 80,
  `query` at most 200, `within` at most 80 (a label like "lia share 11"). A later call replaces it; returns how many were kept.
  `<message>` gains `"ranked": null | { "title", "query", "within", "hits": [ { "post_id", "score": <number|null>, "why": [...] } ] }`,
  saved in `chat_messages.ranked`; SSE event `ranked` `{ "ranked": ..., "posts": { "<post id>": <post shape> } }` after the run that set it, `done` carries it, and thread
  reads put every ranked post in `posts`.
- **The chat's instructions** gain: for "which of these / in this collection are X", call `search(X, { within })`, cut where the
  scores drop, `look()` at borderline posters before answering, and `rank()` the matches (not `show()`).
- `GET /capabilities` `features.about` is true exactly while search is on and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set; `features.about_sound`
  while the `AI` binding is also there.

### 34.7 App (apple/)

- The detail's "search index" section becomes **"what it's about"** (only with `features.search`): the sentence; a **heard** row
  (the first lines of the transcript and the sound tags; "no sound" / "no speech"); the tags as chips the owner edits (× removes,
  "add a tag" field; saving calls `PATCH /library/about`; "reset" when `edited`); **reads closest to** as bars (word, value);
  **what it's searched by** folded (title, text, poster, model, the index badge and dates from before); **closest videos** as 4
  thumbnails that open the post. Loading, probe pending ("cutting frames…") and "not described yet" states are quiet lines.
- Chat: an assistant message with `ranked` shows it under the text as scored rows (thumbnail, title or name, the `why` words as
  small chips, the score with a bar) with a "within <label>" chip above; tap opens the post; long-press previews like a collage tile.
- Copy lowercase, house style (`Copy+Search.swift`, `Copy+Chat.swift`).

### 34.8 Deploy

Apply migration 0022, then deploy the API (new `AI` binding, new vars `ABOUT_MODEL`; the container image changes for the helper's `/about`).
The first runs probe and describe the library in the background (about 25 probes and as many descriptions per 15 minutes until
done), then the indexer embeds every post again. Then the app release.

## 35. The capystore MCP server (addendum, 2026-10-30; owner: "can we build a capystore mcp? same tools at the agent but exposed over mcp please", "I want to be able to enable the agent to upload videos too please")

Any MCP client (Claude Code, Claude Desktop, other agents) can use the chat AI's tools. There is no second copy of them:
the MCP's tools are the op registry's chat calls (section 33), with the same zod checks, handlers and daily caps, so a
new or changed op shows up in both at once. Code: `src/mcp.ts` (protocol, tool list, upload tokens) and
`src/registry/ops/mcp.ts` (the routes and `upload.begin`). Tests: `test/mcp.test.ts`.

### 35.1 The endpoint

- **`POST https://api.capybaraharmony.com/mcp`**: MCP Streamable HTTP, stateless. Each POST is one JSON-RPC message (or a
  batch of up to 20) answered with `application/json`; a notification gets a bodyless `202`. No `Mcp-Session-Id`, no SSE:
  `GET /mcp` and `DELETE /mcp` answer `405` (`allow: POST`). Bodies over 1 MB are `413`.
- **Methods**: `initialize` (answers the client's protocol version when it is one of `2025-11-25`, `2025-06-18`,
  `2025-03-26`, `2024-11-05`, else the newest; capabilities `{ tools: { listChanged: false } }`; server `capystore`; short
  instructions), `ping`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`. Anything else is `-32601`.
- **Auth**: a client key from the web UI, as `Authorization: Bearer <uuid>` (what MCP clients send) or `Api-Key <uuid>`.
  Missing, malformed, unknown or revoked keys get the gate's `401` codes (`error.api.auth.key.*`). A request with an
  `Origin` other than the web app's is `403 error.mcp.origin` (DNS rebinding). There is no OAuth, so clients that only
  do OAuth (claude.ai's custom connectors) cannot add it yet; Claude Code and any client with custom headers can:
  `claude mcp add --transport http capystore https://api.capybaraharmony.com/mcp --header "Authorization: Bearer <key>"`.

### 35.2 The tools

- **The list**: `registry.toolsFor("chat", { search, box, mcp: true })`, the same switches as the chat (meaning search
  while the Space is configured, the workbench box while it is on), minus `show`, `collage` and `jobs.onDone` (they only
  mean something inside the chat app's reply), plus the MCP-only ops (`when: (f) => f.mcp === true`; so far
  `upload.begin`). Names are the call names with `.` as `_` (`library_list`, `make_webp`, `box_run`).
- **Schemas**: each tool's `inputSchema` is its op's zod input as JSON Schema (`io: "input"`); arguments are the op's
  input object as is (no positional arguments). A failed check is a tool error (`isError`) naming the fields. A refusal
  (`OpError`) is a tool error with the op's message, as the chat's model reads it.
- **`look`** returns the posts' pictures as MCP `image` content (fetched and base64'd, at most 4, each at most 4 MB),
  each after a text line with its URL; a picture that cannot be fetched leaves the URL.
- **`ask` ops** (deletes, revokes, making things private, the store switch) run at once: an MCP client has its own
  approval step. They carry `annotations: { destructiveHint: true, readOnlyHint: false }` and their description says to
  check with the user first.
- **Who they run as**: each client key has one MCP "thread", `mcpThreadId(key id)` = 16 base62 characters of
  SHA-256(`mcp:` + key id). Calls run as that thread's chat key (`chat:<thread>`, caller `chat`), exactly like a chat
  thread: the Durable Object sees that key id, the daily caps (saves 33.7, renders 33.8) count per key, and the box keeps
  that thread's files while awake. Results are the op's JSON as text, cut at 200 000 characters.

### 35.3 Uploading a local file: `upload_begin` + `PUT /mcp/upload/<token>`

JSON-RPC cannot carry a video, so the agent gets a link and sends the bytes itself.

- **`upload_begin({ name?, title?, public?, queue? })`** (MCP only, tier free) answers `{ upload_url, method: "PUT",
  expires_at, max_bytes, types, curl }`. The link works for 15 minutes (`UPLOAD_TOKEN_TTL_MS`), for any number of files.
  `curl` is ready to run: `curl -sS -T FILE '<upload_url>'`.
- **`PUT /mcp/upload/<token>`** (auth `capability`): the token is `<base64url claims>.<base64url HMAC-SHA256>`, claims
  `{ k: client key id, t: MCP thread, e: expiry ms }`, signed with a key derived from `COBALT_API_KEY` (rotating it voids
  every link). An expired, forged or tampered token is `401 error.mcp.upload_link`; a token whose client key was revoked
  since is `401 error.api.auth.key.invalid`; nothing is read or stored. Otherwise the body goes through
  `PUT /studio/upload`'s own code (section 3): same types, sniffing of a body with no type (what `curl -T` sends), the
  100 MB cap, `?name`, `?title`, `?public`, `?queue`, the same answer (the item, and for a video the studio session id),
  as the thread's chat key. Logged in `request_log` as `PUT /mcp/upload`.
- A link on the web is saved with `library_save` instead (the save path, 33.7).

### 35.4 Deploy

One API deploy. No D1 migration, no secret, no binding. The route snapshot gained the rows of `/mcp` and two upload
paths; nothing else changed.

### 35.5 The private link (addendum, 2026-10-30; owner: "can we support private urls that have a key in the url to get into? which i can rotate and enable and of course it will like disable the public url")

claude.ai's custom connectors take only a URL (no header, and no OAuth here), so the key can live in the URL instead.
D1 migration `0023_mcp_links.sql`; code `src/mcp-link.ts`, the routes in `src/registry/ops/mcp.ts`; tests
`test/mcp-link.test.ts`.

- **The mode** (D1 `mcp_settings`, one row): `public` (the default, and what a missing row or table means, so the API
  behaves as before until 0023 is applied): `POST /mcp` with a client key, as in 35.1. `private`: only
  `POST /mcp/k/<secret>` answers; `/mcp` is `404 error.mcp.not_found` even with a good key. `off`: both are that 404.
- **`POST /mcp/k/<secret>`** (auth `capability`, the secret is 32 base62 characters, ~190 bits): the same server as
  35.1-35.3 with no header at all. Any secret that is not the live link while the mode is `private` (wrong, old,
  malformed, mode off) is the same `404`; the `Origin` check of 35.1 holds. `GET`/`DELETE` are `405`. Every private link
  runs as one MCP thread, `mcpThreadId("private-link")`, so caps and box files carry over a rotation; its calls carry
  the client id `link:<link id>`, which its upload links name.
- **The link** (D1 `mcp_links`): only the SHA-256 of the secret is kept, plus its last 4 characters (`tail`). At most one
  is live (a partial unique index). It is answered once, as `link_url`, by the call that made it. Each POST records
  `last_used_at` and counts `calls` per UTC day; an `initialize` records `clientInfo.name` as `used_by` (<= 60 chars).
- **`GET /mcp/settings`** (auth `key_or_service`: the app's client key, or the web Worker after Cloudflare Access):
  `{ mode, public_url, link: null | { hint, tail, made_at, last_used_at, used_by, calls_today } }`. `hint` is the link
  with the secret cut to `…<tail>`. The secret is never in it.
- **`POST /mcp/settings` `{ mode }`**: `400 error.mcp.mode` for anything but `off`, `public`, `private`. The same mode
  again changes nothing. Turning `private` on makes a new link and answers `{ settings, link_url }`; leaving `private`
  revokes the link for good (turning it on again makes another). Otherwise `{ settings }`.
- **`POST /mcp/settings/rotate`**: a new link, the old one stops at once: `{ settings, link_url }`. `409
  error.mcp.not_private` outside `private`.
- **Owner only**: the three settings ops are tier `hidden` (not chat or MCP tools) and refuse a chat key (`403
  error.mcp.owner_only`), so an agent can never open or move its own way in.
- **Upload links follow the mode**: a key's upload link (35.3) works only while the mode is `public`, a private link's
  only while that link is live and the mode is `private`; otherwise `404 error.mcp.not_found` (mode) or `401
  error.api.auth.key.invalid` (rotated), and nothing is stored.
- **Web** (`/api/mcp`, `/api/mcp/rotate` on the web Worker, `deploy/cloudflare/web/src/mcp.ts`): Cloudflare Access for the
  owner, the web origin for POSTs, then forwarded through the service binding. Shown on settings > instances.
  **App**: settings > mcp (`McpSettingsScreen`).
- **Logs**: the request URL (with the secret) can appear in Cloudflare's own Workers logs, which only the owner's account
  reads; `request_log` never records it. Rotate if a link may have leaked.
- **Deploy**: migration 0023 (owner), then API and web deploys, then an app release. No secret, no binding.

## 36. Private share links (addendum, 2026-10-30; owner: "private urls that have a key in the url to get into, which i can rotate and enable, and of course it will like disable the public url", then "Alright implement" on the plan `private-links/PLAN.md` and mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/MqXeZHqbrZ7pyBHyJwbjFo)

A post's file (section 22) or a gallery link (section 24) can have a **private link**: `<share_base_url>p/<key>`, `<key>`
22 base62 characters (~131 bits). While it is live the public address of the same thing is a 404, and the private link
is the only way in: its page, Discord's embed and the files themselves are all served under `/p/<key>/`. Rotating
makes a new key and the old one is a 404 at once. Not the MCP's private link (35.5), which stays as it is.

### 36.1 Storage (migration `0024_private_links.sql`)

- `private_links`: `key` (primary key, the 22 characters, stored as is: it opens one link and nothing else, so the
  app can always show and copy it, unlike 35.5), `kind` (`item` | `set`), `target_id` (a `media_items` id or a
  `share_sets` id), `created_at`, `revoked_at` (NULL = live), `opened` (page GETs, not HEAD, not file fetches),
  `last_opened_at`. A partial unique index keeps at most one live row per (`kind`, `target_id`). Rotated rows stay,
  revoked.
- `share_settings` (one row, id 1): `new_links` `public` | `private` (default `public`; a missing row or table means
  `public`), `updated_at`.
- Until 0024 is applied every route below answers `503 error.api.generic` (the GET answers `{ links: [], new_links:
  "public" }`), the share host answers `/p/*` with a 404, and everything else behaves as before.

### 36.2 What "private" means

- **A file** (`kind: "item"`) has three states, which the app calls its link: `off` (not public, no live private
  link; today's "private"), `public` (today's public: `share…/<name>` and the media URL work), `private` (a live
  private link; the file is NOT public: turning it on runs the section 16 OFF first, so the public mirror in
  `cobalt-media` is deleted and the media URL and `share…/<name>` are 404s). Making the file public (the section 16
  route, or 36.3) revokes its private link. Only files the section 16 toggle accepts (`visibility_toggle: true`) can
  have one.
- **A gallery link** (`kind: "set"`) is `public` (`share…/g/<id>`, as section 24) or `private` (a live private link:
  `/g/<id>` and its status and oEmbed routes are 404s). A private gallery link may hold files that are not public: its
  files are served through its key whatever their own state (deleted files drop out as before). A public one still
  shows only public files. Revoking a gallery link (`DELETE /share/sets/<id>`) revokes its private link too.
- A file going private (any way) drops out of PUBLIC gallery links, as today. The app offers to make those links
  private as well (36.6); the server does not do it on its own.

### 36.3 Routes (no-store, CORS for the web app; while the share host is off `404 error.share.off`)

Auth (built 2026-10-30): the `/share/private*` routes take the app's client key (`Authorization: Api-Key`) or the web
Worker's library service credential (`x-cobalt-service`, the owner's web library behind Cloudflare Access, like
`/mcp/settings` in 35.5); `POST /share/collections/<id>/privacy` is keyed only, like the rest of section 26 (the service
gets 404). They are tier `hidden` ops: the chat and MCP reach private links only through 36.5.

`<link>` = `{ "kind": "item"|"set", "target_id", "url": "<share_base_url>p/<key>", "created_at": <ms>, "opened": n,
"last_opened_at": <ms>|null }`.

- `GET /share/private` → `200 { status, links: [<link>...], new_links, sets: [<set>...] }`, every live link, newest
  first, plus the gallery links exactly as `GET /share/sets` lists them (the web Worker's service credential may call
  this route but not `/share/sets`).
- `PUT /share/private/items/<item id>` → `200 { status, link }`: turns the file's private link on (OFF first when it is
  public). Already on: the same link, unchanged. `404 error.private.not_found` (no such live file), `409
  error.private.not_toggleable`, `503`.
- `PUT /share/private/sets/<set id>` → the same for a live gallery link (`404 error.private.not_found`).
- `DELETE /share/private/items/<id>` | `/sets/<id>` → `200 { status }`: the link is revoked. The file stays not public
  (`off`); the gallery link is public again at `share…/g/<id>`. None live: still 200.
- `POST /share/private/items/<id>/rotate` | `/sets/<id>/rotate` → `200 { status, link }`: a new key, the old one a 404
  at once; title, caption and the gallery link's id are kept. `404 error.private.not_found` when none is live.
- `POST /share/private/rotate-all` → `200 { status, links, rotated: n }`: every live key rotated in one batch.
- `PATCH /share/private/settings { "new_links": "public"|"private" }` → `200 { status, new_links }` (`400
  error.private.bad_request`).
- `POST /share/collections/<id>/privacy { "private": bool }` or `{ "rotate": true }` → `200 { status, links:
  [<link>...] }` for every live gallery link in the collection: `private: true` turns on the ones that are not,
  `false` revokes them (public again), `rotate` rotates the live ones. `404 error.collection.not_found`, `400
  error.collection.bad_request`.
- `POST /share/sets` takes optional `"private": bool` (absent: `new_links`). Private: the items must be live files
  (any state), so `409 error.share.not_public` lists only missing or deleted ids; the set is created and its private
  link made in the same call.
- Set shape (section 24) gains `"private": bool`, `"public_url": "<share_base_url>g/<id>"`, `"link": <link>|null`;
  in a private set's `items`, `url` and `poster_url` are the file's and its poster's addresses under `/p/<key>/` (36.4),
  whatever the file's own state (`url` null for a deleted file, `poster_url` null without a poster);
  `url` is the link that works now (the private one while private), so every older client copies the right link.
- Library file shape (`itemShape`) gains `"private_url": string|null` (the live private link of that file).
- `GET /capabilities`: `features.private_links` true exactly with `features.share_sets`.
- Rerolling a collection (26) keeps a private gallery link private: the old key is revoked with the old link and the
  new link gets a new key.

### 36.4 The share host

- `GET|HEAD /p/<key>`: a live key → the page. An item: the section 22 page (title, caption, Open Graph, Twitter
  player). A set: the section 24 page (component embed for `Discordbot`, Open Graph fallback), its files in order.
  Every file and poster URL in it is under `/p/<key>/`; no media-domain URL appears. Anything else (unknown, revoked,
  malformed, the item public again, the set revoked, no files left) is the same plain 404. A GET counts in `opened`.
  `cache-control: no-store` (a rotate takes effect at once), CSP allows only the share origin, `noindex`.
- `GET|HEAD /p/<key>/<n>.<ext>`: file `<n>` (1-based, an item is 1; a set's `<n>` is the file's place in the link, so it
  never changes and a deleted file's number stays a 404) streamed from R2 (`cobalt-media` when its bytes
  live there, else `cobalt-originals`), `<ext>` must be the file's own. `Range` is honoured (`206`,
  `content-range`, `accept-ranges: bytes`; unsatisfiable `416`), `content-type` the file's, `cache-control: private,
  no-store`, `x-content-type-options: nosniff`. `GET|HEAD /p/<key>/<n>.poster.jpg`: the file's poster (its object in
  `cobalt-media`), 404 when it has none. Other methods `405`. Every `/p/` 404 is `no-store`. Code:
  `src/share-private.ts` (the page builders of sections 22 and 24 with the file URLs mapped), `src/private-links.ts`.
- Discord: its crawler and media proxy fetch these URLs like any other; a message posted before a rotate keeps the
  preview Discord saved and stops playing once Discord fetches the file again.

### 36.5 Chat and MCP

- `links.create` (`links_create`) takes `private?: boolean` and answers the live URL (and `private: true` when it
  is one). Absent: a gallery link follows `new_links`; one file stays its public page (only `private: true` turns a
  file's private link on, which makes the file not public).
- `links.private` (`links_private`): `{ link, private: boolean }`, `link` a gallery link id or URL, a `/p/<key>` URL,
  a file id or a post with one file; tier `ask` when turning one off (the public link comes back) else `auto`
  (`free`). `links.rotate` (`links_rotate`): `{ link }`, tier `ask`. Owner-only ops stay hidden: `rotate-all` and the
  settings (and every `/share/private` route).
- `links.list` shows `private` and the live `url` per link.
- Direct file links: wherever a private link comes back (`links.create` with `private`, `links.private` turning one on,
  `links.rotate`, a private link in `links.list`) it also carries `file_urls`, each file's
  `share.../p/<key>/<n>.<ext>` (the bytes, 36.4) in the link's order, null for a file that is gone. `library.post`
  gives a file with its own live private link `private_url` and `private_file_url` (`.../p/<key>/1.<ext>`). These
  stay on the share host, never the media domain; like the page, anyone holding one can download the file until the
  key is rotated or revoked.
- Code: `src/private-actions.ts`, `src/registry/ops/private.ts`; tests `test/private-links.test.ts`,
  `test/private-links-chat.test.ts`, `test/migration-0024.test.ts`.

### 36.6 Clients

- **Post detail** (iPhone, iPad, Mac): the file's link section is `off` · `public link` · `private link` (mockup
  "iphone · a post's link"). Private shows the link, copy, opened count, key made, `rotate key…` (confirm). Turning
  private on while the file is in public gallery links asks: `make those links private too` / `leave it out of them`.
- **Links tab**: each gallery link shows a `public`/`private` badge and its live URL; actions `make private`, `make
  public`, `rotate key…`, `revoke…`. A collection's `privacy…` menu: make every link private, rotate every key, make
  every link public. Copy-all copies live URLs.
- **Make a gallery link**: a `public` / `private` choice, defaulting to `new_links`; with private, files that are not
  public may be picked.
- **Settings › sharing**: `new links start` public / private; `rotate every key…`.
- **Web** library: the same per-file link control and a links view (mockup "web").
- Every place that hands out a link (copy, share sheet, keyboard, Live Activity, Shortcuts) gives the live one: a
  file's `private_url` when set, else its share link.
- **Deploy**: migration 0024 (owner), then API and web deploys, then an app release. No secret, no binding.

## 37. Hidden posts and auto-hide rules (addendum, 2026-10-30; owner: "a way to have set up rules for videos that automatically [are] ... not shareable and they're hidden, and I can manually", then "I want to be able to select videos and have them in bulk be hidden. And also just all statuses like public, private, etc. And also agent needs to have the tools. And also the MCP too"; plan `hidden-posts/PLAN.md`, mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/KKkNoJVvx16zwzRVjyVwHg)

A **post** (the `GET /library` post key) can be **hidden**: it leaves the library and can never be shared until it is
unhidden. Hidden is a post-level state on top of each file's section 16/36 state; a hidden post's files are always
`off` (not public, no live private link). The owner hides by hand (one post, or many at once with the bulk status
route 37.4), and **rules** (37.3) hide new saves automatically. The chat AI and the MCP have the same powers (37.6).

### 37.1 Storage (migration `0025_hidden.sql`)

- `media_hidden`: `post_key` (primary key), `state` `hidden` | `exempt` | `held`, `by` (`you` | `rule` | `chat`; who set
  the state), `rule_id` (the rule that hid it, NULL otherwise), `at` (ms). `hidden` = hidden. `exempt` = the owner (or
  the chat) unhid it: no rule hides it again (the owner can still hide it by hand). `held` = waiting on an `about`
  rule (37.3). No row = a normal post.
- `hidden_rules`: `id` (16 base62), `kind` `site` | `words` | `about` | `upload`, `value` (TEXT, normalized: lower
  case, trimmed; `''` for `upload`), `enabled` (0|1, default 1), `hits` (posts this rule has hidden, counted when it
  hides one), `created_at`, `updated_at`. At most 50 rules.
- Until 0025 is applied: every route below answers `503 error.api.generic`, `GET /library` and every other read behave
  exactly as before (no post is hidden), and `features.hidden` is false. Checked in `sqlite_master` per database
  binding: a yes is kept for the isolate's life, a no is asked again after 60 s (so an isolate that outlives the
  migration picks it up).

### 37.2 What "hidden" means (enforced on the server, every path)

- **Hiding** a post (any way): the row says `hidden` FIRST (so every read leaves it out from that moment), then every
  file of it runs the section 16 OFF when public (public mirror deleted, edge purge), its live private link (36) is
  revoked, its loop previews (29, `media_loops` rows and their R2 objects) are deleted, and a save session of it still
  `public_state: "pending"` gets `null` (it will never publish). Privacy first: if an OFF fails the post stays hidden and
  the answer reports the file under `failed`; a retry finishes it. A public file that cannot be toggled (16):
  - a legacy host copy (`source 'host'`, public bucket) whose save session's original is a live row in the private
    bucket with its object present is **deleted** (public object first, then the row, the edge cache and the session's
    `public_url`); the post keeps its original, and nothing fails;
  - any other (a host copy with no original, or whose original's object is missing; a legacy file that is not a webp)
    is not safe to delete: it stays, reported under `failed` with `error.library.not_toggleable`, and the post is hidden
    anyway (reads leave it out, but **its public URL keeps working** until the owner deletes the file).
- **Reads that leave hidden posts out**: `GET /library` (unless `hidden=1`, below), `GET /library/search`, the sky map
  (`library.map`), `GET /studio/recent`, the about/collection search (34), stage pools (30: a hidden file never shows
  on stream), the roast/caption cron (25) and loops (29) skip them, and the chat/MCP `library.list` /
  `library.search` / `library.query` / `chat.rank` (37.6). Counts (`counts.posts/files`) leave them out too, also in a
  `hidden=1` answer; `counts.hidden` gives the number of hidden posts (always present, 0 before 0025). Hidden posts stay
  in the search index (`media_embeddings`); the search answers drop them, so unhiding needs no re-index.
- `GET /library?hidden=1` (same paging and `v`): only the hidden posts, newest first. `hidden` takes only `0` or `1`
  (anything else is `400 error.library.bad_request`); before 0025 `hidden=1` is an empty page. Each post in any
  `GET /library` answer gains `"hidden": bool` and `"hidden_by": "you"|"rule"|"chat"|null` and
  `"hidden_rule": <rule id>|null`. There is no JSON single-post route; the per-file reads the app opens a post with from
  the album (`GET /library/items/<id>/file`, the poster, `GET /library/about?key=`) keep answering for a hidden post.
- **Nothing can share it**: making a hidden post's file public (16.2 PATCH visibility on), turning its private link on
  (36.3 PUT), adding its files to a gallery link (`POST /share/sets`, collection reroll) all answer `409
  error.hidden` (`{ ids: [...] }` with the hidden file ids for the set route). `POST /studio` with `public: true` for a
  post that a rule hides at save time simply never publishes (37.3).
- **Gallery links** (24, 36): a hidden file is treated like a deleted file everywhere a set is read (public page,
  private page `/p/<key>/<n>` 404, oEmbed, `GET /share/sets` `items` entry with `url: null` and `hidden: true`; the
  key is absent for a file that is not hidden). Unhiding does not
  make it public, so a public set still leaves it out; a private set shows it again (accepted).
- **Nothing is made of it**: starting a webp render (`POST /studio/<sid>/render`), a slideshow or still
  (`/slideshow`), a gallery image (`/gallery-image`) on a hidden post's session, or an editor render
  (`POST /edits/<id>/render`) with a clip from a hidden post, answers `409 error.hidden` (the editor adds `ids`, the
  hidden clips' file ids) before anything is queued. The chat/MCP `make.*` calls and `edits.render` refuse the same
  way before taking a unit of the daily cap, with the hint of 37.6. Backstop for a job that was already running when its
  post was hidden: a webp render is recorded off (its bytes move to the private bucket, the public object is deleted,
  the answer's and the render row's `url` are `null`; if that fails the public object is deleted and the row dropped);
  a slideshow, still or gallery image is recorded private and never made public (section 16's ON refuses a hidden post).
  An editor render is always a new private post of its own. Reopening a hidden post's file in the studio
  (`POST /library/items/<id>/studio`) still works; what is made there is refused as above.
- **Unhiding**: the row becomes `exempt` (`by` who unhid it). Every file stays `off`; nothing is published again.

### 37.3 Rules

Matched against a post when its save first becomes ready (the place section 13's `public: true` publish runs, BEFORE
publishing), and on `apply` (backfill). A post whose row is `exempt` or already `hidden` is skipped. Matching:

- `site`: `value` is a host (`instagram.com`) or host + path prefix (`tiktok.com/@name`, `x.com/name`). Matches when
  the post's source link's host equals the host or ends with `.<host>` (`www.`, `m.`, `vm.` all count; `twitter.com`
  and `x.com` are the same site) and, when a path is given, the link's path starts with it (case-insensitive, `/`
  boundary: `x.com/name` does not match `x.com/names`).
- `words`: `value` (1..60 chars) appears, case-insensitive, in the post's custom title (15), its file name, its roast
  title (25, when written) or its source link.
- `about`: `value` (1..40 chars) is one of the post's about tags or sound tags (34, `user_tags` replacing `tags` as
  everywhere), or a whole word (or whole-word phrase) of its sentence or transcript. The words come minutes after the
  save, so: while at least one enabled `about` rule exists, a save that asked for `public: true` is NOT published at
  ready; its post row becomes `held` and its session keeps `public_state: "pending"` (an image upload answers
