  `public_state: "pending"` too). The 15-minute cron (after the about writer, 34.3) releases a held post once its
  words are in (`written_at` or `user_tags` set) or the writer gave it up (probe `failed`, or 3 failed writes; a probe
  `none` post still gets its words from the title and caption), or 60 minutes since it was held, or at once when the
  about writer is off (no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) or no enabled `about` rule is left. On release the rules run again: a
  match hides it, otherwise the row is deleted and the section 13 publish runs then (the session's `public_state`
  becomes `ready` or `failed`). A `held` post is listed normally as `off` and can be shared by hand (making a file
  public, turning its private link on, putting it in a gallery link, or a bulk `off`/`public`/`private` deletes the
  `held` row: the owner decided).
- A save that a rule hides at ready (any kind but a held `about`) never publishes: its session's `public_state` becomes
  `null` and its `public_url` stays `null`.
- `upload`: the post's source is `upload` (any file uploaded: Photos, Files, share sheet with a file, MCP upload).
- Matching values: `site` takes an optional scheme and `www.`, which are dropped (`https://www.X.com/Name/` is stored
  `x.com/name`; `twitter.com` is stored `x.com`); a value with no dot in the host, or a space, is `bad_rule`.
  `words` and `about` are trimmed, lower-cased and have inner whitespace collapsed.

The first matching enabled rule (oldest first) is the one recorded. Its `hits` goes up by one.

### 37.4 Routes (keyed `Authorization: Api-Key`, or the web Worker's library service credential like 36.3; no-store, CORS for the web app)

`<rule>` = `{ "id", "kind", "value", "enabled", "hits", "created_at", "updated_at" }`.

- `POST /library/status { "ids": [<file or post ids>, 1..200], "status": "off"|"public"|"private"|"hidden" }` →
  `200 { status: "success", posts: [{ "id": <the id sent>, "post_key", "status": <new>, "files": [<itemShape>...] }],
  failed: [{ "id", "error": "<code>" }] }`. Ids naming files of the same post count once (the post answers under the
  first id sent). Per post, every toggleable file (`visibility_toggle`):
  - `hidden`: 37.2 hiding (`by` `you`, or `chat` from the chat/MCP).
  - `off`: unhide if hidden (row → `exempt`), then section 16 OFF and revoke private links.
  - `public`: unhide if hidden, then section 16 ON (revokes private links, as 36.2).
  - `private`: unhide if hidden, then 36.3 PUT for each file (a file's own private link; a gallery link is not made).
    Without a share host (`SHARE_BASE_URL` unset) the post goes to `failed` with `error.share.off`.
  A `held` row (37.3) is dropped by `off`, `public` and `private`. Ids that are unknown or deleted go to `failed`
  (`error.library.not_found`); a post with no toggleable file to `failed` (`error.private.not_toggleable` for
  `private`/`public`; `hidden` and `off` still work). `400 error.library.bad_request` for a bad body. Posts are
  processed with at most 4 in flight.
- `GET /hidden/rules` → `200 { status, rules: [<rule>...], hidden: <count of hidden posts>, held: <count held> }`,
  rules oldest first. `created_at`/`updated_at` are ms; `enabled` is a boolean.
- `POST /hidden/rules { kind, value, enabled?, apply?: bool }` → `201 { status, rule, applied: n }`; `apply: true`
  runs the rule over every saved, not-exempt, not-hidden post and hides the matches (`applied` = how many). `400
  error.hidden.bad_rule` (unknown kind, empty or too long value, `site` not a host; also any other bad body on this
  route), `409 error.hidden.too_many` (the 51st). `apply` on a rule created with `enabled: false` applies nothing.
- `POST /hidden/rules/preview { kind, value }` → `200 { status, matches: n, sample: [<post_key>...<=6] }` (what
  `apply` would hide now; nothing changes).
- `PATCH /hidden/rules/<id> { enabled?, value?, apply? }` → `200 { status, rule, applied }`. `404 error.hidden.not_found`
  (also for an id that is not 16 base62), `400 error.hidden.bad_rule` for a bad `value`.
- `DELETE /hidden/rules/<id>` → `200 { status }`, `404 error.hidden.not_found`. Posts it hid stay hidden.
- `GET /capabilities`: `features.hidden: true` once 0025 is applied (false before), and `limits.status_ids: 200`
  (always present).
- The refusals of 37.2 (`409 error.hidden`) answer `{ status: "error", error: { code: "error.hidden" } }`, plus
  `ids` on `POST /share/sets` and the collection reroll.

### 37.5 Discovering the state in clients

The library post gains `hidden`, `hidden_by`, `hidden_rule` (37.2). A file's link mode stays as 36 (`off` / `public`
/ `private`); a hidden post shows `hidden` instead, and its other modes are greyed with "unhide first".

### 37.6 Chat and MCP (ops in `registry/ops/hidden.ts`; MCP gets them as tools like every chat op)

- `library.status` (`library_status`): `{ ids: string[] (1..50), status: "off"|"public"|"private"|"hidden" }`, the
  bulk route with `by: "chat"`. Tier: `hidden` and `off` run at once (they only take things out of view); `public` and
  `private` ask (confirm card), and `off` asks too when any target is hidden (it unhides). In the registry the op is
  tier `ask` with a conditional `asks(input, ctx)` (it may be async); the MCP marks it `destructiveHint` and runs it
  directly, like every ask op. Answers `{ status, posts: [{ id, files: <count>, links: [<working URLs>] }], failed }`.
- `library.hidden` (`library_hidden`): `{ limit?: 1..50 (default 50), cursor? }` → `{ posts, next }`: the hidden posts,
  most recently hidden first, in the `library.post` shape plus `hidden_by`, `hidden_rule`, `hidden_at` (ISO); `next`
  is the cursor for the page after (the last post key of this page) or `null`. Hidden posts are otherwise invisible to `library.list`/`search`/`query`/`map`/`chat.rank`; `library.post`
  on a hidden post id answers it with `hidden: true` (so the AI can tell why it can't share it).
- `hidden.rules.list` (`hidden_rules_list`; the chat also accepts `hidden.rules()`): `{ rules, hidden, held }`, free. (A
  call cannot be named `hidden.rules` because `hidden.rules.*` is its namespace.) `hidden.rules.add`
  (`hidden_rules_add`): `(kind, value, { apply? })` (always enabled), runs at once without `apply`, asks with it (the MCP marks
  it `destructiveHint` either way). `hidden.rules.update` (`hidden_rules_update`) `{ id, enabled?, value?, apply? }` and
  `hidden.rules.delete` (`hidden_rules_delete`) `{ id }`: `ask`. `hidden.rules.preview` (`hidden_rules_preview`): free.
- `make.webp`, `make.still`, `make.slideshow`, `make.galleryImage`, `make.trim`, `make.crop`, `edits.render` (and a
  `jobs.wait` that starts an opened file's make) on a hidden post answer `error.hidden` with the same hint.
- `links.create`, `links.private`, `library.visibility` (turning a file public) on a hidden target answer the
  `error.hidden` refusal with a hint: "it's hidden; unhide it first with library.status".
- The owner's route ops (`library.status.route`, `hidden_rules.*`) are tier `hidden`: never chat or MCP tools.
- The existing `library.visibility` op stays (old prompts) and calls the same code.

### 37.7 Clients

- **Library** (iPhone, iPad, Mac, web): a `hidden` filter chip last (black when on). On iPhone/iPad/Mac it is locked
  with Face ID / Touch ID / the device passcode each time it is opened (`LAContext`, `.deviceOwnerAuthentication`);
  Settings › hidden › "lock with face id" (on by default, stored on the device). Hidden posts never show elsewhere:
  all, kinds, search, sky map, shelves, recent links, widgets, keyboard, Photos sync, Spotlight.
- **Select mode** (library and the hidden album): a `status…` action (menu: hidden, off, public link, private link)
  applies to every selected post with one `POST /library/status`; confirm for hidden ("their links stop working for
  good") and for public. In the hidden album the action is `unhide` (→ `off`) plus the same menu.
- **Post detail**: the link section gets a fourth row `hidden` (37.5); hidden shows "hidden by you / rule <value>" and
  `unhide`.
- **Settings › hidden**: rules list (kind label, value, hits, on/off switch, swipe to delete), `add rule…` sheet (kind,
  value, live preview count from `/hidden/rules/preview`, "also hide what's saved" switch = `apply`), lock switch,
  `open hidden`.
- Offline copies of a hidden post stay on the device but are listed only in the hidden album.
- **Deploy**: migration 0025 (owner), then API and web deploys, then an app release. No secret, no binding.

## 38. Rich tool cards in chat (addendum, 2026-10-30; owner: "I should be able to see images, editors and emotes the agent uses all in rich ui including edit timeline and we can further add more rich ui for the tooling the agents get please"; plan `tool-cards/PLAN.md`, mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/4ZgrUg4a6EuwZro4PvomxS)

A chat code step (27.1) used to show only its code, logs and result. Now every `cobalt.*` call the run makes is recorded on
the step, and the calls with something to see carry a card the app draws under the step. Additive: no migration, no route,
no secret; MCP (35) is unchanged; older apps ignore the new field and event. The chat lives only in the apps (the web
library has no chat), so only the apps draw cards.

### 38.1 The calls

`Step.calls?: ToolCall[]` (kind `code` only; absent on older messages), in call order, at most 40 per step, saved inside
`chat_messages.steps`:

```
ToolCall = { op: string;            // the op name, "library.search"
             ms: number;            // how long the call took
             status: "done" | "error" | "asked";   // asked: an ask-tier call that put up a confirm card (33.5)
             error?: string;        // status error: the line the model got (cut to 300 characters)
             card?: ToolCard }      // only for calls with something to see
```

The chat toolbox (`registry/ai.ts`) records each call after it ends; cards are built by `src/chat-cards.ts` from the op's
checked input and its result and never reach the model. `edits.update` (and `edits.addOverlay`, section 39) first read the
project so the card can mark the clips the call added. A card that cannot be built is left out (a chip only).

### 38.2 The cards

Every card has `type`. Lists are cut (8 hits, 24 emotes, 12 posts, 60 clips per track; box stdout keeps its last 1,500
characters, stderr 800, the command 600). Posts are named by id only; 38.3 ships their shapes.

| type | from | fields |
|---|---|---|
| `search` | library.search, library.list | `query`, `within` (a collection name, link id or "N posts"; null), `total`, `hits: {post_id, score|null}[]` |
| `look` | look | `post_ids` |
| `emotes` | emotes.find, emotes.save | `query|null`, `sort|null` (section 20.1's `top_weekly`, `trending_daily`...), `saved`, `emotes: {id, name, animated, url (https), library_id|null}[]`; accepts the old bare array and 20.1's `{sort, page, has_more, items}` |
| `edit` | edits.get, edits.create, edits.update, edits.addOverlay | `project_id`, `title`, `revision`, `duration`, `width`, `height` (the canvas), `change: created|updated|viewed`, `tracks: {name: "main"|"overlay", index, clips: EditClip[]}[]` (main first, then one per overlay layer by `track`, lowest first), `render: {status, progress, post_id}|null` |
| `job` | make.*, edits.render, jobs.wait, jobs.onDone | `op`, `job`, `status`, `progress`, `error`, `post_id`, `kind`, `seconds`, `width`, `height`, `bytes`, `share_url`, `watching`, `project_id` (make.trim/crop) |
| `box` | box.run | `cmd`, `code`, `timed_out`, `stdout`, `stderr` |
| `files` | box.pull (`direction: "in"`), box.save (`"out"`) | `files: {name, bytes}[]`, `post_id` (the pulled id / the new post) |
| `link` | links.create, links.rotate, links.private | `id|null`, `url`, `title`, `files`, `private` |
| `collection` | collections.create/add/update, sets.create/add/update | `of: "collection"|"set"`, `id`, `name`, `count` (links / collections), `change: created|added|updated` |
| `saved` | library.save | `post_ids`, `title`, `status` |
| `facts` | library.title, library.visibility, library.about.set | `title`, `rows: [name, value][]`, `post_ids` |

`EditClip = { id, item, post_id|null (the file's post, for its thumbnail), kind: video|photo|gif|webp|emote|cutout|null
(from the library file: source emote, a name with "cutout", else its content type; null when the file is gone), title,
picture (a public picture: an emote's or image's own URL, else a poster; null), at, length (seconds on the output
timeline; main clips back to back, length = (out - in) / speed), in, out, speed, pitch|null, is_new }`.
`is_new` is true only when the card saw the timeline before the call and the clip id was not in it.

Any other op: a chip only. A new op gets a card by adding a case to `cardFor` (`src/chat-cards.ts`).

### 38.3 Thumbnails

- Stream: just before a code step's final `step` event, `event: card_posts`, `data: { posts: { <id>: <post> } }` with the
  `GET /library?v=3` shapes of every post the step's cards name (hidden posts left out, 37.2). The app merges them into the
  thread's map; they are not shown posts (`post_ids` is unchanged). Each call also re-sends its step (`step` event) as it
  ends, so chips appear while the run goes on.
- `GET /chat/threads/<id>`: `posts` also holds every post the messages' cards name.

### 38.4 App (apple/)

- `ChatStep.calls: [ChatToolCall]`, `ChatToolCard` (lossy: an odd call, card or list element is dropped; an unknown card
  type is a chip only), `ChatEvent.cardPosts`. `ChatStep.cards` folds cards of the same thing (a job's later state, a link,
  a collection, an edit; a plain look at an edit never hides the card that showed what changed).
- Under each code step: chips (op, time; red when failed, amber "asks first"), then the cards: search (thumbnails with score
  bars), looked at, 7TV emotes playing (`EmotePicture`, a tap names one), edit (ruler, main + overlay rows, pictures dashed,
  speed badge, added clips ringed violet, tap a clip for its times, drag the red playhead, "open in editor"), job (progress,
  then the made post), box (terminal, exit code, show all), files, share link (copy, open), collection/set, saved, facts.
- Previews: the canned reply's step carries one of every card (`PreviewData.chatToolCalls`).

## 39. Editor layers, webp/emote sources and cutouts (addendum, 2026-10-30; owner: "put images and convert images to remove any white bg or colour bg ... and then have the option to place it over a video I can stack multiple and also I want to be able to overlay multiple videos can we also expand our like agent and mcp tools too please"; plan `editor-layers/PLAN.md`, mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/VqimMVfpEKT12kxKEkLhqb)

Section 31's timeline gets **layers**: overlays sit on up to 8 tracks, so pictures, cutouts, 7TV emotes and videos can
overlap in time and stack. Pictures can be **cut out** (background removed) into a new private PNG in the library. The
chat AI and the MCP get `make.cutout` and `edits.addOverlay`. No migration, no secret.

### 39.1 Overlay `track` (timeline, `editor/helper/timeline.js`)

- An overlay may carry `track`: an integer `0..7` (`EDIT_OVERLAY_TRACKS = 8`). Absent = `0`. A non-integer answers
  `error.edit.bad_request`, out of range `error.edit.range` (with the usual `path`).
- Canonical JSON omits `track` when it is `0`, so every timeline saved before this section is byte-identical (same
  fingerprint, same cached render).
- The section 31 "overlays never overlap" rule now holds **per track**: two overlays on different tracks may overlap
  freely. `overlay[]` stays sorted by `at` (as before).
- Draw order (`overlayDrawOrder`): track ascending, then array order. A higher track is drawn on top. The render
  (`compile.js`) and the app preview use the same order.
- Every overlay video's sound is mixed (one `amix` input per unmuted overlay video plus the main track), as before.
- `GET /capabilities` `limits.edit_overlay_tracks: 8`. A client that does not know the limit sends no `track`, which
  keeps the old one-lane behaviour.

### 39.2 webp sources (7TV emotes, animated webps)

- `EDIT_SOURCE_TYPES` gains `image/webp`; the Worker treats it as a `photo` source (`sourceKind`). Library files saved
  by the 7TV search (`emotes.save`, source `emote`) and webps made by `make.webp` can be overlays and clips.
- Render container (`editor/helper/probe.js`, new `webp.js`): a webp is sniffed by its bytes (`RIFF....WEBP`). A still
  webp is a picture like a PNG (alpha kept). An **animated** webp is parsed in JS (VP8X + ANMF frames), each frame is
  decoded by ffmpeg to RGBA and composed per the WebP spec (blend/dispose, frame offsets), and the result is written as
  an APNG (`anim.png`) the compile reads with `-f apng` and loops like a gif (section 31 gif rules: it loops for the
  overlay's length). Frame durations ≤ 10 ms count as 100 ms (browser rule). Limits: 2000 frames, canvas 4096 px a
  side, 400 M pixels in total; past them (or a broken file) the render fails with `error.edit.unsupported_source`.
- The render image's build checks that ffmpeg has the `webp` and `apng` decoders and the `apng` demuxer.

### 39.3 `POST /library/items/<id>/cutout` (client key; op `library.cutout`, tier `hidden`)

`<id>` is a picture file (`image/png`, `image/jpeg`, `image/webp`, `image/gif`, first frame) the caller can see. The
answer is a **new** library item: a private PNG in `cobalt-originals` at `cutouts/<new id>.png`, source `studio`,
`made_from: [<id>]`, `made_spec: {kind: "cutout", mode, ...}`, title "<source title> cutout" (or the given `title`). The
source is never changed.

Two bodies:

- **JSON** (or empty) `{ mode?, color?, tolerance?, edges_only?, soften?, trim?, title? }`, the server makes it:
  - `mode`: `auto` (default; lifts the subject off any background with Cloudflare Images `segment: "foreground"`),
    `white` (takes out white), `color` (takes out `color`, `#rgb` or `#rrggbb`, required for this mode).
  - `tolerance` 0..100 (default 20; colour distance as a percent of black-to-white), `edges_only` (default true: only
    background connected to the picture's edge goes, so white eyes stay), `soften` 0..3 px (default 1), `trim`
    (default true: crop to what is left). `tolerance`/`edges_only`/`soften` apply to `white`/`color` only.
  - The source is first scaled to at most 2048 px a side (and turned into PNG) through the `IMAGES` binding; a PNG
    source in `white`/`color` mode is read directly when it is small enough. The flood fill and PNG encode run in the
    Worker (`src/cutout-core.ts`).
- **`content-type: image/png`** (the app's own on-device cutout, iOS Vision): stored as is after a PNG check, at most
  20 MB and 8192 px a side; query `?title=&mode=` (mode recorded in `made_spec`).

Answers: `201 { status: "success", item, title, cutout: { width, height, mode } }`. Errors: `404 error.cutout.not_found`,
`409 error.cutout.not_image`, `400 error.cutout.bad_request` (+ `path`), `413 error.cutout.too_large` (source over
30 MB), `422 error.cutout.empty` ("nothing was left"), `502 error.cutout.failed` (Images failed), `502
error.cutout.storage`, `503 error.cutout.off` (no `IMAGES` binding and the source needs it).

`GET /capabilities` `features.cutout: true` (the route exists; device PNG uploads always work) and
`features.cutout_auto` (= the `IMAGES` binding is present: server `auto` and non-PNG sources work).

Cost: Cloudflare Images bills each unique transformation; background removal is in open beta (5,000 free a month on
the account). A cutout is made only on request, never in the cron.

### 39.4 Chat AI and MCP ops (`registry/ops/editor.ts`)

- `make.cutout` (`make_cutout`, tier free): `{ id, mode?, color?, tolerance?, edges_only?, soften?, trim?, title? }`; `id`
  is a picture file id or a post id (its first picture). Counts against `CHAT_DAILY_RENDERS` (refunded on failure),
  refuses hidden posts (section 37), answers a done job `cutout:<new id>` with the new item, so `jobs.wait` works
  like the other makes.
- `edits.addOverlay` (`edits_addOverlay`, tier free): `{ project, file, at?, seconds?, in?, track?, position?, x?, y?,
  size?, opacity?, muted?, volume? }`. `file` is a video, gif, photo, webp/7TV emote or cutout (file id or post id).
  Without `track` it takes the lowest track free over `[at, at + seconds)`; `position` is one of the nine named
  spots (`center` default, margins 4% of the width); `size` is the width as a canvas fraction (default 0.35, a video
  0.4), the height follows the source's aspect. Answers the project like `edits.update` plus `clip` (the new
  overlay's id) and `track`. When every layer is taken over that span it answers `error.make.refused` ("all 8 layers are busy then"); an explicit busy `track` is refused the same way.
- `edits.get` also returns `sources` (each source's id, kind, size, seconds, `source`), so the AI can place things.
- The timeline type text the AI reads (`TIMELINE_TYPE`) documents `track`, webp/emote/cutout sources and points at
  `edits.addOverlay`.
- Flow for "put this emote over the clip": `emotes.find` → `emotes.save` → `edits.addOverlay`; for "cut out the
  background of this sticker and stack it": `make.cutout` → `edits.addOverlay`.

### 39.5 Clients (app plan, `apple/CONTRACT-EDITOR.md` section 12)

The app shows layers as lanes L1..L8 under the main track, an add sheet with videos / images / emotes tabs, and a cutout
sheet (auto / white / colour, tolerance, keep-inside switch, before/after) that makes the cutout on the device and
uploads the PNG through 39.3, or asks the server when the device can't (`features.cutout_auto`).

### 39.6 Files, tests, deploy

- Files: `editor/helper/{timeline,compile,probe,webp}.js`, `editor/Dockerfile`, `api/src/{cutout,cutout-core}.ts`,
  `api/src/registry/ops/editor.ts`, `api/src/app-routes.ts`, `api/src/edits.ts`, `api/cloudflare.config.ts`.
- Tests: `editor/helper/test/layers.test.js` (track parse/canonical, overlap per layer, draw order, webp sources,
  animated webp frame blending and looping through real ffmpeg renders, stacked layers, two video overlays mixed) and
  `api/test/editor-layers.test.ts` (cutout route both bodies and every error, colour flood fill, PNG round trip,
  `make.cutout`, `edits.addOverlay` placement and free-track choice, capabilities).
- **Deploy**: API deploy (new `IMAGES` binding, `bindings.images()`, Images must be enabled on the account) which also
  rebuilds the `EditRenderer` image (webp support). No migration, no secret. Older apps keep working (they never send
  `track`).

## 41. Editing a gallery link's files (addendum, 2026-10-30; owner: "I can edit collection links, but I can't click on the share link and edit the videos in that. So I need UX UIs for all that flow"; plan `share-link-edit/PLAN.md`, mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/Uicw2T9mJKSiZP5RQGcJKv)

A gallery link (section 24) can now be opened and its files added, taken out and moved, and its title changed, after it
was made. Its id and URL never change. No migration: the files were always one JSON list in `share_sets.items`.
Sections 38 to 40 belong to other threads.

### 41.1 The route (`key_or_service`: a client key or the web Worker's library service credential; no-store, CORS)

`PATCH /share/sets/<19 digits>` body `{ "items"?: ["<file id>", ...], "title"?: string | null, "expect"?: ["<file id>", ...] }`
→ 200 `{ "status": "success", "set": <set> }` (the section 24.1/36.3 shape, a hidden file marked `hidden: true` as in
`GET /share/sets`).

- `items`: the whole new list in the order shown, 2..10 distinct library file ids. A file already in the link may stay
  whatever its state now (deleted, private in a public link, hidden: the apps show it as "not shown"). An added file must
  be live and not hidden (`409 error.hidden` with `ids`), and in a public link public now (`409 error.share.not_public`
  with `items`: the added ids that are missing or not public). A private link takes any live file.
- `title`: a string (trimmed, 1..80) sets it; `null` or `""` clears it (the roast caption shows again); absent keeps it.
- `expect`: the list the client last saw. When the stored list differs: `409 error.share.changed` with `set` (the current
  one) and nothing written. The write itself only lands if the list is still the one read (a race answers the same 409).
- At least one of `items` and `title`, else `400 error.share.bad_request` (also: a bad shape, not 2..10, duplicates, a
  title over 80). Revoked or unknown: `404 error.share.not_found`. Share host off: `404 error.share.off`.
- When the files change, the roast caption (section 25) is written again, waiting at most 6 s like creation (the cron
  retries a miss); a typed title still wins on the page. An added video kicks moving previews while GALLERY_LOOPS is "1".
- A private link's file addresses `share…/p/<key>/<n>.<ext>` follow the new order: file `<n>` is whatever is n-th now.
- The share page answers `cache-control: public, max-age=60`; messages already posted in Discord keep the preview Discord
  stored for them.

`GET /capabilities`: `features.share_set_edit` is true exactly while the share host is configured.

### 41.2 Chat and MCP

`cobalt.links.update(link, { add?, remove?, order?, title? })` (tier free, like `collections.update`; an MCP tool like
every chat op). `link` is the id or its share URL. `add`: post ids (or file ids), resolved like `links.create` (a gallery
post brings its public files; in a private link any file), appended in order. `remove`: file ids, or a post id for every
file of that post. `order`: file ids put first in that order, the rest after in their order. Refusals name the fix
(fewer than 2: "revoke it instead"; over 10: "make another link"). Result `{ id, url, title, files, private?, file_ids }`.
The call sends `expect`, so a link changed meanwhile throws "read it again".

### 41.3 Web Worker

`PATCH /api/library/links/<id>` (Access-checked like the rest of `/api/library`) relays the body to `PATCH /share/sets/<id>`
with the library service credential and answers its body and status.

### 41.4 Clients

- **Links tab** (iPhone, iPad, Mac): a tap on a link card opens its screen (swipes and the long press stay as they were).
  Inside a collection too.
- **The link screen**: title, the link with copy share, the public/private badge, its collections; a "how discord shows
  it" preview of the files that show; the files numbered 1..N in order, each with its kind and length, and "not shown:
  private / deleted / hidden" when it no longer shows. A tap on a file opens its post.
- **Edit** (toolbar): drag to move, minus to take out (off at 2 files: "a link needs 2 files. revoke it instead"), "add
  files" (off at 10: "full"), the title as a field, and a bar with the changes ("2 added · 1 out · new order"), cancel and
  save. Leaving with changes asks "discard changes?". Save sends `items` (and `title` when changed) with `expect`; a 409
  `error.share.changed` reloads the link and says so. The toast offers undo (a second PATCH with the old list).
- **Add files**: the library grid with search and kind chips; files already in the link are ticked and locked; in a
  public link private files are dimmed ("private: only in private links"); hidden posts are not listed; the pick numbers
  continue the link's (8, 9, 10) and stop at the room left.
- **Library select bar**: "add to link" opens a list of links (cover, title, n → m files); a link without room, already
  holding every pick, or public while a pick is private is dimmed with the reason. Picking one appends and toasts "added
  to <title>" with "open". The list loads itself when it opens, so it works before the links tab was ever visited.
- **Library context menu** (long press; right click on the Mac; addendum 2026-11-01, owner: "when i right click or
  select a video i need a button to add it an existing share"): "add to link…" on any shareable, not hidden post with a
  file opens the same list for that post (on the Mac table, for the whole selection when the row is part of it).
- **Web library** (links view): a click on a link card opens the same screen as a panel (drag tiles, an x on each, add
  files from the library grid in a dialog, the title field, save/cancel).
- Shown only with `features.share_set_edit`.

## 42. Accounts: sign in through Cloudflare Access instead of pasting a key (addendum, 2026-10-31; owner: "I am wondering instead of having to supply api key can we have user accounts?", then "could we just embed cloudflare access more deeply", "maybe when session expires the app has a persistent button to easily relogin?", "Implement please"; plan `accounts/PLAN.md`, mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/PejJSZT4MKVJkVvurmNsYa)

Cloudflare Access (already in front of `cobalt.capybaraharmony.com`, owner email only) does the logging in. Each
signed-in device then holds its own **device session**: a row in `api_keys` with `kind = 'session'`. Its token has the
exact shape of a client key (lowercase UUIDv4) and is sent the same way (`Authorization: Api-Key <token>`, MCP
`Bearer`), so every route, `key_id` column and MCP/chat identity works unchanged. Owner-only: no users table, no
per-user data, nothing to copy. Today's keys stay valid and are shown as "keys for tools". Section 40 is skipped (another
thread may hold it).

### 42.1 D1 (migration `0026_sessions.sql`)

```
ALTER TABLE api_keys ADD COLUMN kind TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'key';   -- 'key' | 'session'
ALTER TABLE api_keys ADD COLUMN device TEXT;          -- session: "harmony's iphone", "safari on mac" (<= 40 chars)
ALTER TABLE api_keys ADD COLUMN platform TEXT;        -- session: 'ios' | 'ipados' | 'mac' | 'web'
ALTER TABLE api_keys ADD COLUMN email TEXT;           -- session: the Access email that signed it in
ALTER TABLE api_keys ADD COLUMN expires_at INTEGER;   -- session: ms; NULL for keys (never expire)
ALTER TABLE api_keys ADD COLUMN last_country TEXT;    -- cf.country of the last use (both kinds)
ALTER TABLE api_keys ADD COLUMN replaced_by TEXT;     -- key: the session id that took over from this pasted key
CREATE TABLE auth_handoffs (
    code_hash  TEXT PRIMARY KEY,     -- SHA-256 hex of the one-time code
    challenge  TEXT NOT NULL,        -- base64url(SHA-256(verifier)), 43 chars (PKCE S256)
    device     TEXT NOT NULL,
    platform   TEXT NOT NULL,
    email      TEXT NOT NULL,
    created_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
    expires_at INTEGER NOT NULL,     -- created_at + 2 min
    used_at    INTEGER
);
CREATE INDEX idx_api_keys_sessions ON api_keys (kind, revoked_at, last_used_at DESC);
```

A session's `name` is its device, `prefix` the token's first 8 characters (as for keys).

### 42.2 Lookup (API Worker `src/keys.ts`)

`lookupKey` accepts a row only when `revoked_at IS NULL AND (expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > now)`, and in the same
statement sets `last_used_at = now`, `last_country` (when known), and for `kind = 'session'` slides
`expires_at = now + 90 days` (`SESSION_TTL_MS`). An expired or revoked session answers 401
`error.api.auth.key.invalid` like an unknown key: that one code is what the clients treat as "signed out".

At most 50 active sessions: minting one more revokes the least recently used.

### 42.3 Signing an app in (web Worker, behind Access)

1. The app makes a random `verifier` (32 bytes, base64url) and opens, in `ASWebAuthenticationSession` (callback scheme
   `cobalt-apple`, not ephemeral, so Access's cookie is reused next time):
   `https://cobalt.capybaraharmony.com/api/auth/app?platform=<ios|ipados|mac>&device=<name>&challenge=<base64url(SHA-256(verifier))>`
2. Access shows its login page if needed. The web Worker verifies the Access JWT (owner email, as `/api/keys`), checks
   the query (platform from the list, device 1..40 printable characters, challenge 43 base64url characters), stores an
   `auth_handoffs` row with a fresh 32-byte base64url code, and answers **302** to
   `cobalt-apple://signed-in?code=<code>` (fixed scheme and path, never taken from the request). Bad query: 400 page.
3. The app calls the API: `POST /auth/exchange` (open, no CORS) body `{ "code", "verifier", "old_key"? }` →
   200 `{ "status": "success", "key": "<token>", "session": <session> }`. The code must exist, be unused and unexpired, and
   `base64url(SHA-256(verifier))` must equal its challenge; it is marked used in the same statement. Otherwise 400
   `error.api.auth.code.invalid`. `old_key`: the pasted key this device used before; if it is a valid `kind = 'key'` row
   its `replaced_by` is set to the new session id (it keeps working until revoked).

### 42.4 Signing the web app in (web Worker, behind Access)

`POST /api/auth/web` (Access JWT + `Origin` = `WEB_ORIGIN`) body `{ "replace"?: "<old token>" }` → 200
`{ "key": "<token>", "session": <session> }`: a new session, platform `web`, device from the User-Agent ("safari on
mac", "chrome on windows", "firefox on linux", else "browser"). `replace`: if it is a session, it is revoked (the browser
is re-signing itself in). The web app keeps the token where it keeps today's custom api key and calls the API with it.
Access's own cookie is what keeps this possible: when it has run out, the fetch is redirected and fails, and the page
reloads to go through Access.

### 42.5 API routes (registry ops in `registry/ops/auth.ts`; `auth: key` unless said; CORS for the web origin; no-store; tier `hidden`: never chat or MCP tools)

`<session>` = `{ "id", "device", "platform", "created_at", "last_used_at", "expires_at", "country": string | null, "current": bool }`
`<key>` = `{ "id", "name", "prefix", "created_at", "last_used_at", "old_paste": bool }` (`old_paste` = `replaced_by` set)

| route | answer |
|---|---|
| `POST /auth/exchange` (open, no CORS) | 42.3 |
| `GET /auth/me` | `{ "caller": { "kind": "key" \| "session", "id", "device"?, "platform"?, "email"?, "expires_at"? }, "sessions": n, "keys": n }` |
| `GET /auth/sessions` | `{ "sessions": [<session>] }` active only, most recently used first |
| `DELETE /auth/sessions/<uuid>` | revokes it (yours too = sign out) → `{ "status": "success" }`; unknown/already revoked → 404 `error.api.auth.session.not_found` |
| `POST /auth/signout` | revokes the caller if it is a session → `{ "signed_out": true }`; a key is never revoked here → `{ "signed_out": false }` |
| `POST /auth/signout-others` | revokes every other active session → `{ "count": n }` |
| `GET /auth/keys` | `{ "keys": [<key>] }` active `kind = 'key'` rows, newest first |
| `DELETE /auth/keys/<uuid>` | revokes a key (never a session) → `{ "status": "success" }`; else 404 |

Keys are also made on the web (`/api/keys`, which now lists and creates `kind = 'key'` rows only); since section 50
the apps make and rename them too. `GET /capabilities` gains `features.accounts: true`.

### 42.6 Clients

- **Signed out state** (any 401 `error.api.auth.key.invalid`, or no token): the app keeps the current screen, the
  offline files and what's already loaded; actions that need the server are dimmed. A **"sign in again"** button stays
  pinned until tapped: floating bottom-right on iPhone/iPad (above the tab bar), in the window toolbar on Mac, a top bar on
  web. After signing in the app stays on the same screen and reloads.
- **First launch / no token:** the sign-in screen: one "sign in" button (opens 42.3) and a small "have an old api key?"
  that still accepts a pasted key.
- **Move-over:** a device that has a pasted key and no session shows once: "capystore has accounts now" with "sign in to
  switch" (42.3 with `old_key`) and "later". After the switch the pasted key is removed from the keychain.
- **Settings → account** (replaces settings → api key): who's signed in (Access email, "through cloudflare access"),
  devices (42.5 list, sign out each, "sign out all other devices"), keys for tools (list, revoke, "old paste" tag, "make
  keys on the web"), private links note, sign out.
- The share extension and Shortcuts read the token from the shared keychain item exactly as they read the key today.
- Mac TestFlight: if the sandboxed build can't read the old key, it simply shows sign in.

## 43. Sub-agents: the chat AI starts helpers, tracks them, and they message each other (addendum, 2026-10-31; owner: "agents can delegate sub-agents and track them, and they communicate between each other"; plan `sub-agents/PLAN.md`)

Before this, one chat reply did everything itself in at most 8 code runs. Now the chat AI (and an MCP client) can hand
independent parts of a big job to **sub-agents**: more chat AIs (the same model, the same `cobalt` tools in code mode) that
each work on one task in the background and report back. Agents (the parent and its sub-agents, and sub-agents among
themselves) send each other messages while they work.

### 43.1 What a sub-agent is

- A row in D1 `chat_agents` (migration 0027) plus a chat thread with the **same id** (`chat_threads`, `chat_messages`): its
  task is the thread's first user message, each of its replies an ordinary assistant message with steps and tool cards
  (27, 38). The thread is left out of `GET /chat/threads` and of the 200-thread cap, and `POST /chat/threads/<id>/messages`
  to it answers 409 `error.chat.agent_thread` (only its parent writes to it). `GET /chat/threads/<id>` on it adds
  `agent: { id, parent, name, status, task }`. Deleting it deletes its row and mail; deleting a parent thread deletes all
  its sub-agents.
- `parent` is the chat thread that started it, or the MCP thread (35, `mcpThreadId`) of the client key that did.
- `status`: `queued` (started or woken, its reply not begun), `running`, `done`, `error` (its reply failed; `error` holds
  the code), `stopped` (by `agents.stop`). `result` is the text of its last reply (its report); `post_ids` the posts that
  reply showed; `activity` the op of its latest call while running; `turns` the replies it ran.
- One level only: a sub-agent cannot start sub-agents. At most 4 queued or running per parent (`error.chat.agents_busy`,
  429), at most 20 started per parent per UTC day (`CHAT_DAILY_AGENTS`, default 20, `"0"` turns sub-agents off;
  `error.chat.agents_cap`, 429; the counter is the main Durable Object's chat budget, 33.8, kind `agents`). A parent keeps
  at most 30 sub-agents: starting one more drops its oldest finished ones. Each sub-agent runs at most 6 replies (its task,
  then up to 5 wakes by messages).

### 43.2 Where it runs

The `ChatAgent` Durable Object (`src/chat-agents-do.ts`, rules in `src/chat-agents.ts`), one per sub-agent
(`idFromName(<agent id>)`), bound as `CHAT_AGENTS`. The Worker reaches it only with `PUT /wake {"agent": id}` and
`x-internal-key`; it sets its alarm, and the alarm runs the agent's next reply (`runAgentTurn` in `src/chat.ts`): the reply
code of 27 with the deps of the chat's own replies (the model through `ModelRelay`, code in the Worker Loader sandbox). The
reply runs inside the alarm, never after a response. When it ends with mail still unread, the alarm runs again at once.
Without the binding (or with `CHAT_DAILY_AGENTS = "0"`) no `agents.*` call is offered. Before migration 0027 the calls
answer 503 `error.chat.agents_not_ready` and everything else behaves as before.

### 43.3 The calls (chat and MCP; tier free, no route)

| Call | Input | Answers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `cobalt.agents.spawn(task, { name?, posts? })` | task 1..4000 chars; name 1..40 (default `helper <n>`, unique per parent, case-insensitive); up to 20 post ids, handed with the task | the `Agent` (43.4) |
| `cobalt.agents.list()` | | `{ agents: Agent[], unread }` (`unread`: the caller's own unread mail) |
| `cobalt.agents.get(agent)` | id or name | `Agent` plus `calls`: the ops of its latest reply's calls |
| `cobalt.agents.send(to, text)` | a sub-agent's name or id, or `"parent"` from a sub-agent; text 1..2000 | `{ sent, to, to_name, woke }` |
| `cobalt.agents.inbox()` | | `{ messages: AgentMail[] }`: the caller's unread mail, now marked read |
| `cobalt.agents.stop(agent)` | id or name | the `Agent` (parent only) |

- `spawn` is offered to the parent only. A sub-agent's calls run as its parent's chat key (`chat:<parent>`): the same daily
  caps (renders, saves), the same studio sessions and the same workbench box (33.11). It never gets confirm cards: an `ask`
  op throws ("a sub-agent cannot ask") and the agent puts it in its report, so Harmony still answers one card in the main
  chat. `jobs.onDone` only looks for a sub-agent (it has no watcher); it may `jobs.wait`.
- `send` to a sub-agent that is `done` or `error` (and has replies left) sets it `queued` and wakes it: it answers in a new
  reply. A running one reads it before its next model turn. Sending to a stopped one is 409 `error.chat.agent_stopped`.
  Mail to `"parent"` waits for the parent: a running parent reply reads it before its next turn; otherwise `agents.inbox`
  or the app (43.6).
- **Mail between turns**: before each model turn after the first, and before the first when unread mail is waiting, the
  reply takes the caller's unread mail (at most 10, oldest first), marks it read and adds it to the model's turn as one note
  (`[New message (cobalt.agents): - from sub-agent "<name>" (<id>): ...]`). It is saved with the reply, so the thread replays.
- **System prompts**: the parent's gets a "Sub-agents" paragraph while `spawn` is in its API (split big jobs with parts that
  do not depend on each other, each task standing alone, hand each job to `jobs.onDone` and end the reply, make the `ask`
  calls a report asks for itself). A sub-agent's starts with who it is (`"<name>", a helper started by the main chat`), that
  its final reply is a plain report to the parent (no kaomoji), to `show` the posts that matter, and how to message.

### 43.4 Jobs and the `Agent` shape

- An agent's job id is `agent:<id>`. `jobs.wait` and `jobs.onDone` (33.14) take it; only its parent and its siblings may
  (else "is not one of this chat's sub-agents"). The state: `queued`/`running` (with `activity`), `done` (`result`,
  `post_ids`), `error` (`error`, `result`), `cancelled` for stopped. `JobSchema` gains the optional `agent`, `name`,
  `activity`, `result`, `post_ids`. So the parent hands its helpers to `jobs.onDone`, ends its reply, and the ChatJobs
  watcher continues the chat as each one finishes; the continuation's note asks the model to relay the report.
- `Agent`: `{ agent, job, name, status, task, activity, result, post_ids, error, turns, unread, created_at, updated_at,
  ended_at }` (`activity` only while running; times in ms).
- `AgentMail`: `{ id, from, from_name, to, to_name, text, created_at, read }` (`from`/`to`: `"parent"` or an agent id).

### 43.5 Tool cards (38)

A new card `{ type: "agents", change, agents, message, mail }`: `change` is `started` (spawn), `listed`, `viewed` (get),
`stopped`, `sent`, `inbox` or `waiting` (`jobs.wait`/`jobs.onDone` on an agent job, instead of the job card). `agents`:
up to 8 rows `{ id, name, status, task (≤200), activity, result (≤300), post_ids (≤12), watching }` (`stopped` for a
cancelled job). `message`: `{ to, text }` for `sent`; `mail`: `[{ from, text }]` for `inbox`. Its post ids join the
message's `posts` map.

### 43.6 The app

- **`GET /chat/threads/<id>/agents`** (keyed, web CORS, no-store): `{ agents: Agent[], mail: AgentMail[] }` (the last 50
  messages between the thread and its sub-agents, oldest first). 404 `error.chat.not_found` for an unknown thread; before
  the migration, empty lists.
- The thread screen shows the **helpers** as chips at the top of the reply that started them (the `agents` card of its
  `started` call; a helper no card names sits on the newest reply): one chip each (name, status dot, `activity` while running), refreshed every 3 s while any is `queued` or `running` (and after each reply's
  `done` event), at most every 30 s otherwise. Tapping a chip opens the sub-agent's thread read-only (no composer), with its
  task as the first bubble and a footer for its status; the mail with the parent is listed at the top of that sheet.
- The `agents` card draws the rows with the same chips; `sent` shows an arrow line "to <name>: <text>"; `inbox` the
  messages.
- Copy (lowercase): "helpers", "working", "waiting", "done", "stopped", "didn't finish", "asked", "to <name>",
  "from <name>", "this helper works for the chat, you can't write here".

### 43.7 Deploy

D1 migration 0027 (two tables, two indexes), then one API deploy: a new Durable Object class and binding (`CHAT_AGENTS`
→ `ChatAgent`, sqlite) and the var `CHAT_DAILY_AGENTS` in `cloudflare.config.ts`. No secret. The route snapshot gains
`/chat/threads/<id>/agents` rows only.

## 44. Cropping pictures and using them in videos (addendum, 2026-10-31; owner: "if I give a full image crop editing tools that an agent can use, and also the user can use for images on the service ... I say, look at this video, look at this screenshot, crop the screenshot, and then use this element in the screenshot in the video"; plan `image-crop/PLAN.md`, mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/TpWXqfmaoEuFLBsGA5Fua9)

Any picture in the library can be **cropped** (a box in fractions of the picture), turned a quarter turn, flipped and,
optionally, have its background removed (section 39) in one step. The result is a **new** private PNG in the library,
linked to the picture it came from, usable anywhere a picture is (an editor overlay, a gallery, a share link). The chat AI
and the MCP get `make.cropImage`, and `look` learns to see private pictures, video frames and a grid. No migration, no
secret, no new binding. (Section 43 is the sub-agents thread's.)

### 44.1 `POST /library/items/<id>/crop` (client key; op `library.crop`, tier `hidden`)

`<id>` is a picture file the caller can see (`image/png`, `image/jpeg`, `image/webp`, `image/gif` first frame,
`image/avif`, `image/heic`; the section 39.3 list). The source is never changed. The answer is a new item: a private PNG in
`cobalt-originals` at `crops/<new id>.png`, source `studio`, `made_from: [<id>]`, `made_spec: {kind: "crop", from, crop,
rotate, flip, cutout?}`, title "<source title> crop" (or the given `title`).

Two bodies, like 39.3:

- **JSON** `{ crop?, rotate?, flip?, cutout?, title? }`, the server makes it:
  - `crop`: `{x, y, w, h}`, fractions of the source picture (top-left origin, before any turn): `0 ≤ x, y < 1`,
    `0 < w, h ≤ 1`, `x + w ≤ 1.001`, `y + h ≤ 1.001` (clamped to the edge). Absent = the whole picture. The box is
    rounded outward to whole pixels and must be at least 2 × 2 px.
  - `rotate`: `0` (default), `90`, `180`, `270`: degrees **clockwise**, applied after the crop.
  - `flip`: `"none"` (default), `"h"` (left to right), `"v"` (top to bottom), applied after the turn.
  - `cutout`: `false` (default), `true` (= `{mode: "auto"}`), or a section 39.3 spec `{mode, color?, tolerance?,
    edges_only?, soften?, trim?}`: the background is removed from the cropped picture, then it is trimmed to the subject
    (unless `trim: false`).
  - At least one of `crop`, `rotate ≠ 0`, `flip ≠ "none"`, `cutout` must be given.
  - The source is decoded through the `IMAGES` binding (scaled down to at most **3072 px** a side, which keeps a phone
    screenshot at full size); without the binding a PNG source of at most 3072 px a side is read directly, anything else
    answers `503 error.crop.off`. The crop, turn and flip run in the Worker; `cutout.mode: "auto"` sends the cropped PNG
    through `IMAGES` once more (`segment: "foreground"`).
- **`content-type: image/png`** (the app cropped on the device at full resolution): stored as is after a PNG check, at most
  20 MB and 8192 px a side; query `?title=&x=&y=&w=&h=&rotate=&flip=&cutout=` is recorded in `made_spec` (with
  `device: true`); any of it may be left out.

Answers: `201 { status: "success", item, title, crop: { width, height } }` (the result's pixel size). Errors:
`404 error.crop.not_found`, `409 error.crop.not_image`, `400 error.crop.bad_request` (+ `path`: `crop`, `crop.x`,
`rotate`, `flip`, `cutout`, `cutout.mode`, `title`, ...), `413 error.crop.too_large` (source over 30 MB, upload over
20 MB), `422 error.crop.empty` (the cutout left nothing), `502 error.crop.failed` (Images failed or the picture can't be
decoded), `502 error.crop.storage`, `503 error.crop.off`.

`GET /capabilities` `features.image_crop: true` (the route is there; the app's own PNGs always work; server crops of
non-PNG pictures and `cutout.mode: "auto"` need `features.cutout_auto`).

### 44.2 `make.cropImage` (chat AI and MCP `make_cropImage`, tier free)

`cobalt.make.cropImage(id, crop?, { rotate?, flip?, cutout?, title? })`: `id` is a picture file id or a post id (its first
picture); `crop` is `{x, y, w, h}` as 44.1. Refuses hidden posts (section 37), counts against `CHAT_DAILY_RENDERS`
(refunded on failure), and answers a done job `crop:<new id>` with `file_id`, `post_id`, `kind: "image"`, `width`,
`height`, `public: false`, `share_url: null`, like `make.cutout`. `make.crop` given a picture says to use `make.cropImage`.
The chat shows it as a job card (section 38).

### 44.3 `look` sees more (chat AI and MCP)

`cobalt.look(ids, { grid?, frames? })` (MCP `look { ids, grid?, frames? }`):

- **Private pictures**: a post whose picture is not public (an upload, a cutout, a crop, an original kept private) is read
  from R2, scaled to at most 1568 px a side through `IMAGES` (a PNG or JPEG that is small enough goes as is) and sent to
  the model as base64. Before this, `look` only saw public pictures and said "has no picture to look at".
- `grid: true`: the picture gets thin lines every tenth of its width and height (thicker at the half), so the model can
  read off a crop box in the fractions 44.1 takes. The model is told so in the text before the picture.
- `frames: true`: for a video, up to 3 frames spread across it (the stored "what it's about" frames of section 34, when
  there are any) after its poster.
- Still at most 4 posts per reply (chat) or per call (MCP); each picture at most 5 MB.

### 44.4 The flow the chat is taught

"Use part of a picture in a video": `look([video], {frames: true})` and `look([picture], {grid: true})` → `make.cropImage(
picture, {x, y, w, h}, {cutout: true})` when only the thing itself is wanted → `look([file_id])` to check the crop, again
with a better box if something is cut off → `edits.addOverlay(project, file_id, {...})` (with `edits.create` first when
there is no project for the video yet) → `edits.render` only when Harmony wants it.

### 44.5 Clients (app; `apple/CONTRACT-EDITOR.md` section 13)

A crop sheet on iPhone, iPad and Mac: the picture with a crop box (drag to move, corners to resize), aspect chips `free`,
`original`, `1:1`, `4:5`, `9:16`, `16:9`, rotate left, flip, `remove background`, and `save a copy` / `crop and place`.
It opens from a picture in the library (viewer and context menu), from a picture in the editor's bin and add sheet, and
from an image overlay (the crop then replaces the overlay's picture: same place, width, time and layer). The app crops on
the device at full resolution and uploads the PNG (44.1 second body); when the picture isn't on the device it asks the
server (44.1 JSON). Shown only when `features.image_crop` is true.

### 44.7 `make.frames`: video frames as pictures (owner, same day: "a tool for agents to extract and analyze frames of a video too and then they can edit them as image")

`cobalt.make.frames(id, { at?, count?, title? })` (MCP `make_frames`, tier free, offered only while the workbench box is on,
section 33.11): `id` is a video or gif file id, or a post id (its first video). `at` lists seconds (at most 8, each inside
the video); without it `count` frames (default 1, at most 8) are spread evenly, each in the middle of its share. The video
is copied into the thread's box and ffmpeg cuts each frame (`-ss <t> -frames:v 1`, at most 3072 px a side, never scaled
up); each becomes a new private PNG in `cobalt-originals` at `frames/<new id>.png`, source `studio`, `made_from: [<video>]`,
`made_spec: {kind: "frame", from, at}`, title "<video title> frame m:ss.s". Answers `{ frames: [{ file_id, at, width,
height }] }`. One unit of `CHAT_DAILY_RENDERS` per call (given back on failure); refuses hidden posts. The frames are
pictures like any other: `look` (with a grid), `make.cropImage`, `make.cutout`, `edits.addOverlay`. For a quick glance
`look(ids, { frames: true })` (44.3) needs no box and makes no files. The chat's instructions (44.4) mention it when the box
is on.

### 44.8 Cropping without guessing (addendum, 2026-10-31, after a live job where the chat cropped a video "centred on her" and colour-cut a banner against a black shirt)

- `look(ids, { box })` (MCP `look { box }`): `box` is `{x, y, w, h}` in fractions, as 44.1. It is drawn on every picture
  `look` sends for those posts (the picture and, with `frames`, each frame): outside darkened, a cyan edge. The text before
  each picture says so. Nothing is made and nothing is spent: it is how the model checks a box before `make.cropImage` or
  `make.crop`.
- The `grid` is labelled: the digits 1..9 (tenths) sit along the top edge right of each vertical line and down the left
  edge under each horizontal line, white on a dark backing.
- `look(ids, { frames: true })` on a video with no stored frames says to use `make.frames`.
- The chat's instructions (44.4) become a cropping routine for pictures and videos: see the real source with the grid (a
  video's frames, not its poster; `make.frames` when there are none or the subject moves), read the box with a margin,
  check it with `look({ box })`, cover a moving subject in every frame looked at (or split the clip into several main
  clips with their own crops), use `cutout` only for a subject on a plain background and never for banners, bars, text or
  other rectangles, look at the result after, and ask Harmony one short question when the subject is unclear. The MCP
  instructions and the `make.crop` / `make.cropImage` summaries say the same in short. `make.cropImage` stays a plain crop
  unless `cutout` is given.

### 44.6 Files, tests, deploy

- Files: `api/src/{image-crop,image-crop-core,look-pictures}.ts`, `api/src/registry/ops/{editor,frames}.ts`,
  `api/src/registry/ops/library.ts`, `api/src/{chat,chat-tools,chat-cards,mcp,app-routes}.ts`.
- Tests: `api/test/image-crop.test.ts` (both bodies and every error, crop/rotate/flip pixels, cutout chaining,
  `make.cropImage`, `look` private pictures, grid and frames in chat and MCP, capabilities).
- **Deploy**: API deploy only. No migration, no secret, no binding change.

## 45. Chat videos: a chat replayed as an MP4 (addendum, 2026-10-31; owner: "create like a video, like animation of the chat with the video embedded, and it plays it"; plan `chat-video/PLAN.md`, sample `chat-video/sample-chat.mp4`)

A chat thread (section 27) rendered as a video that replays it: the owner's message types into the composer and is sent,
the reply "thinks", its tool chips and cards (section 38) pop in one by one, its text streams in word by word, then the
posts it showed appear and their videos play inline with their sound. The camera scrolls like the app. The result is a
private library post, made by the editor's render containers (section 31.8) as a second kind of render.

### 45.1 Route

`POST /chat/threads/<id>/video` (keyed, CORS like the other chat routes, no-store), body (all optional, at most 4 KB):

```
{ "shape": "portrait" | "landscape" | "square",   // 1080x1920 (default), 1920x1080, 1080x1080; 30 fps, H.264 + AAC
  "theme": "dark" | "light",                       // default dark
  "speed": 0.5..2,                                  // pacing (typing, popping, streaming); default 1. Videos play at 1x
  "from": "<message id>", "to": "<message id>",    // only these messages, both included (ids from GET /chat/threads/<id>)
  "notify": true | false }                          // one Hark message when it ends (31.11), default false
```

→ `202 { "status": "success", "render": <render> }` (31.12's shape, 45.3). Errors: `400 error.chat.bad_request` (bad
JSON, unknown key, bad value; `from`/`to` not a message of the thread), `404 error.chat.not_found`, `404 error.chat.off`,
`404 error.edit.off` (no render containers), `409 error.chat.busy` (a reply is being written), `409
error.chat.video.empty` (nothing to show: no user or assistant message in the range), `429 error.edit.queue_full`.
`GET /edits/renders/<rid>`, `DELETE /edits/renders/<rid>` (cancel), `GET /edits/renders` and `GET /queue` show and
cancel it like any render.

### 45.2 What is in it

- Messages in order, at most 40; user and assistant only. Per assistant message: each code step's header ("ran code · N
  tools · 0.8 s"), its chips, then at most 6 cards in all (38.2's types: search, look, emotes, edit, job, box, files, link,
  collection, saved, facts; thumbnails are the posts' posters), the text (markdown: bold, code, links), then up to 4 of the
  posts it showed. A user message's attached posts show as pictures.
- A shown video post plays from its start for at most 12 s with its sound (faded out at the end); a picture shows for a
  moment. Pacing at speed 1: typing 28 characters a second (at most 2.2 s a message), replies 14 words a second (at most
  5 s of streaming). The video is at most 180 s; past that the chat is cut at a message.
- Hidden posts (section 37.2) are left out, like in thread reads. Code, logs and results of steps are never drawn.
- Files used: each shown post's first video or picture and every post's poster (a picture without a poster uses itself).
  At most 50 files and 6 GiB (31's caps); posters past that are left blank.

### 45.3 The render

- `edit_renders` gets `kind` (`'edit'` | `'chat'`, default `'edit'`) and `thread_id` (migration 0028). A chat render's
  `project_id` is `"chat:<thread id>"` (never a project), `revision` 0, `title` the thread title, `timeline` the frozen
  spec (45.4), `seconds_total` the planned length.
- `<render>` gains `kind` and `thread_id` (null for editor renders). Older apps see an extra render in the queue whose
  project does not open.
- The result: a private post in `cobalt-originals` (`edits/<rid>.mp4`, source `studio`, `made_from` the files used,
  `made_spec: {kind: "chat", thread, render}`), titled with the thread title, poster from 10 % in (31.10).
- Progress is 31.12's: `seconds_done` counts drawn seconds. Rendering takes about 2.5x the video's length.

### 45.4 The spec and the container

The Worker freezes the thread when the render is queued: `{ "kind": "chat", "thread_id", "options": {shape, theme,
speed}, "snapshot": {...}, "inputs": [{n, item, bucket, key, bytes, kind}] }`. `snapshot` is the thread read
(`{version: 1, thread: {id, title}, messages: [{id, role, text, status, post_ids, steps: [{id, kind, status, duration_ms,
calls: [{op, ms, status, card}]}]}], posts: {<id>: {id, title, kind, poster: "<n>"|null, files: [{kind: "video"|"image",
url: "<n>"|null, width, height, duration, share_url}]}}}`), with every file named by its input number `n`. The renderer
streams the inputs like an editor render (31.9), then `POST /renders/<rid>/start {"chat": {snapshot, options}, "inputs":
[{n, item, kind}]}`. The helper (`editor/helper/chat/`) draws the chat in headless Chromium (`page.html`, at half the
output size, 2x) one frame at a time, pipes the JPEG frames to ffmpeg (libx264 CRF 20, veryfast), then mixes the played
videos' sound in at their moments. A missing input is drawn without its picture.

### 45.5 The chat AI and MCP

`cobalt.chat.video({ thread?, shape?, theme?, speed?, from?, to? })` (MCP `chat_video`, tier free): `thread` defaults to
the calling chat (MCP: required). One unit of `CHAT_DAILY_RENDERS` (refunded when it is refused); answers a job (`edit:<rid>`,
33.8) for `jobs.onDone` / `jobs.wait`; the job card is 38.2's `job`.

### 45.6 Capabilities, clients, files

- `features.chat_video`: `chat` and `editor` are on and migration 0028 is applied.
- App (`apple/`): "make video" in a chat thread's menu opens a small sheet (shape, theme, speed, "only from this message"
  from a message's menu); the render shows in the queue and opens the library post when done.
- Files: `editor/helper/chat/{plan,page,render,cdp}.js`, `page.html`, `page.css`, `fonts/` (IBM Plex Mono, OFL);
  `editor/Dockerfile` (Chromium, Noto emoji and CJK fonts); `api/src/chat-video.ts` (snapshot, inputs, queue),
  `api/src/edit-render-core.ts` (the `chat` kind), `api/src/edits.ts` (shape), `api/src/registry/ops/chat.ts` (route and
  op); `d1/migrations/0028_chat_videos.sql`.
- **Deploy**: migration 0028, then the API (the editor image is rebuilt: Chromium makes it about 400 MB bigger).

## 46. Shared chats: a chat as a web page (addendum, 2026-10-31; owner: "a way to export the chats in a website ... I can share them"; mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/9rpGmXSkizGbGpcofKhcaR. Numbering: 45 + 0028 belong to the chat-to-video thread)

Chats stay private until the owner shares one. Sharing makes `share.capybaraharmony.com/c/<key>` (22 base62 characters,
stored readable like a private link's, 36): the chat up to the message it was shared at, its tool cards (38) drawn as
plain HTML, and the videos and pictures it shows playable on the page. At most one live link per chat; rotating makes a
new key and the old one is a 404 at once; stopping revokes it; deleting the chat revokes it too.

### 46.1 Storage (migration 0029, `chat_shares`)

`key` (PK), `thread_id`, `upto_id` (the last message on the page), `media` (1: files play; 0: titles only), `code` (1: the
model's code, folded), `files` (JSON: the lead `media_items` ids the link streams, in page order, at most 300),
`created_at`, `updated_at`, `revoked_at`, `opened`, `last_opened_at`. Partial unique index: one live row per thread.
Before the migration the owner routes answer 503 and `/c/*` is a 404.

### 46.2 Owner routes (keyed, CORS for the web origin, no-store; 404 `error.share.off` while the share host is off)

- `GET /chat/threads/<id>/share` → `{status, share: Share | null}`; unknown chat 404 `error.chat.not_found`.
- `PUT /chat/threads/<id>/share` `{media?: bool, code?: bool, upto?: "latest"}` → 201 `{status, share}` when it makes the
  link (up to the latest message that is not a reply still being written; media on, code off unless given), else 200
  with the live share changed. `upto: "latest"` moves the page (and its file list) up to now. An empty chat is 409
  `error.chat_share.empty`; a bad body 400 `error.chat_share.bad_request`.
- `POST /chat/threads/<id>/share/rotate` → `{status, share}` with a new `url`; none live: 404 `error.chat_share.not_found`.
- `DELETE /chat/threads/<id>/share` → `{status}`; none live: 404 `error.chat_share.not_found`.
- `Share`: `{url, thread_id, upto_id (string), upto_at, messages (on the page), behind (written after it), files, media,
  code, created_at, updated_at, opened, last_opened_at}`. Hidden from the chat AI and MCP (tier hidden).

### 46.3 The share host

- `GET|HEAD /c/<key>`: the page. Plain HTML and CSS, no script; dark and light by `prefers-color-scheme`. Open Graph:
  `og:title` = the chat's title, `og:description` = "a chat · N messages · N videos" and the first question, `og:image` =
  the first poster or picture on the page (`summary_large_image`). `cache-control: no-store`; CSP `img-src`/`media-src`
  the share host and the media domain (plus `https://cdn.7tv.app` for emote cards); `noindex`. A GET counts an open.
- `GET /c/<key>/chat.json`: the transcript the page is drawn from (`chatTranscript()` in `api/src/chat-share.ts`):
  `{version: 1, thread {id, title, created_at, shared_at, url}, media, code, messages [{id, role, text, created_at, status
  ("done" | "error"), post_ids, ranked, collage {title, query, total, post_ids (first 24)}, steps [{kind, status,
  duration_ms, code (null unless code is on), calls [{op, ms, status, card}], confirm {op, summary, status}, job {note,
  status}}]}], posts {<post id>: {id, title, kind, files, duration, file {n, type, content_type, url, poster, width, height,
  duration} | null}}, counts {messages, videos, pictures}}`. `access-control-allow-origin: *`. Step logs and results are
  never in it.
- `GET|HEAD /c/<key>/<n>.<ext>` and `/c/<key>/<n>.poster.jpg`: file `n` of the share's list, streamed from R2 with Range
  (as 36.4). 404 when media is off, the file is deleted, its post is hidden since, or the extension is not the file's.
  A public file plays from the media domain instead (cached); the page uses `/c/` only for the rest.
- Every page shows: messages up to `upto_id` (a reply still running is left out), each reply's steps as chips (with the
  code folded when `code` is on) and its cards, its shown posts as players (each post plays once; later mentions are
  thumbnails), ranked lists and collages as thumbnails. Hidden posts (37) are never on it. Share links to private things
  (`/p/<key>`, other `/c/<key>`) in any text or card read "[private link]"; a private link card has no URL.

### 46.4 Apps

The thread menu's "share as web page…" opens the share sheet: not shared yet, an explanation, the two settings and "make
a link"; shared, the link (copy, share, open), "shared up to N messages" with "update" when newer ones came, the
settings (saved at once), the open count, "new link" (confirm) and "stop sharing" (confirm). CobaltKit:
`ChatShare`, `ChatShareChange`, `CobaltClient.chatShare/shareChat/rotateChatShare/stopChatShare`, `ChatStore.loadShare/
share/rotateShare/stopShare/shareOf`.

- Files: `api/src/{chat-share,chat-share-page}.ts`, `api/src/registry/ops/chat-share.ts`, `api/src/share.ts` (the `/c/`
  dispatch), `api/src/chat.ts` (delete revokes), `d1/migrations/0029_chat_shares.sql`; apple `Models/ChatShare.swift`,
  `API/HTTPCobaltClient+ChatShare.swift`, `Store/ChatStore+Share.swift`, `Preview/PreviewChatShare.swift`,
  `Screens/Chat/ChatShareSheet.swift`, `Design/Copy+ChatShare.swift`.
- Tests: `api/test/chat-share.test.ts`; `CobaltKitTests/ChatShareTests.swift`.
- **Deploy**: migration 0029, then the API deploy, then an app release. No secret, no binding change.

## 47. Timed transcripts: subtitles, "said at", and moments by meaning (addendum, 2026-10-31; owner: "is it possible with the Gemma embedding to have the exact time that word was sent, like a transcription? ... as you play the video, you can have, like, enable subtitles, and it, like, appears, under the video", then "implement, please."; mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/Q1WbgggurWdESQsBEkSrCB)

EmbeddingGemma only makes search vectors; it never hears the sound and has no idea of time. Whisper (section 34.3) already
hears every probed video and answers the time of every word, which was thrown away. This section keeps it: subtitle lines
with word times per post, a route for them, `said` on search hits (where the query was said, or the moment that matched by
meaning), and a vector per stretch of speech. Section numbers 45 and 46 (and migrations 0028, 0029) belong to other work.

### 47.1 Lines

- Workers AI `@cf/openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo` answers `segments: [{ start, end, text, words: [{ word, start, end }] }]`
  and `vtt` beside `text`. A segment without `words` has its words spread over it by length; no `segments` at all falls back
  to the `vtt` cues; anything malformed is skipped (`src/transcripts.ts` `segmentsOf`).
- Lines (`linesOf`): words in order, times never running backwards, rounded to 0.01 s. A new line starts at each segment,
  after a pause of 1.2 s or more, after a sentence end once the line holds 24 characters, or before the line would pass
  84 characters or 6 s. At most 800 lines and 6,000 words a post.
- The speech model is the plain var `TRANSCRIBE_MODEL`: `assemblyai` (the default and the deployed value; Workers AI
  `assemblyai/universal-3.5-pro`, about $0.0035 a minute, most accurate; the MP3 goes as a `data:` URI in
  `audio_url`, `language_detection` on; `words: [{ text, start, end }]` in milliseconds, one segment per `utterances` entry
  when present: `segmentsOfAssembly`) or `whisper` (above, about $0.0005 a minute). The writer's `media_about.transcript`
  comes from the same model. A transcript remembers its model, so changing the var hears every post again.
- The probe's sound (34.2) is now the first **600 s** (helper `ABOUT_AUDIO_SECONDS`, 2.4 MB of MP3 at most; the Durable
  Object accepts up to 3 MB). Posts probed before keep their first 180 s until they are probed again.

### 47.2 D1 and the listening pass (migration `0030_transcripts.sql`, additive; the owner applies it before the API deploy)

- `media_transcripts`: one row per post. `post_key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, item_id, probe_at` (the probe heard), `state`
  (`done` | `no_speech` | `failed`), `language`, `text` (the lines' words joined), `lines` (JSON
  `[{ s, e, t, w: [{ t, s, e }] }]`), `model` (`<whisper model>+v1`), `attempts`, `failed_at`, `created_at`, `updated_at`.
- `media_moments`: `(post_key, idx)` primary key, `start`, `"end"`, `text`, `input_hash`, `model`, `dims`, `vector` (47.4).
- The 15-minute cron, with the `AI` binding, inside the "what it's about" run (after the probe kick, before the writer):
  `timeTranscripts` hears up to 40 posts for up to 60 s (3 at a time), newest probe first: probe `done` with sound, and no
  transcript of that probe by this model (a failure is tried again a day later, at most 3 times). It first removes the
  transcripts of posts that are gone or have no sound now. Each success also sets `media_about.transcript` / `language`
  (the plain text, at most 4,000 characters, as 34.3). Every post heard before this section is heard once more this way
  (about 40 a run; under $0.001 each).
- The about writer (34.3) reuses a stored transcript of the same probe instead of calling Whisper again; when it hears first,
  it stores the lines too.

### 47.3 `GET /library/transcript?key=<post key>[&from=<s>&to=<s>]` (op `library.transcript`, `key_or_service`, no CORS, no-store)

```json
{ "status": "success", "post_key": "…", "state": "done", "language": "en", "model": "@cf/openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo+v1",
  "updated_at": 1793443458000,
  "lines": [{ "start": 4.1, "end": 7.9, "text": "sir, you cannot bring the tail into the cabin.",
              "words": [{ "text": "sir,", "start": 4.1, "end": 4.52 }, …] }, …] }
```

- `state`: `done` (lines), `no_speech` (heard, nothing said), `pending` (not heard yet: no probe yet, or a new probe),
  `no_sound` (an image, a probe `none`, or no audio stream), `failed` (3 failures of the probe or of the hearing), `off`
  (would be pending, but there is no `AI` binding). `lines` is empty unless `done`.
- `from` / `to` (seconds) keep the lines that overlap them. A bad number or no key: `400 error.library.bad_request`; an
  unknown post: `404 error.library.not_found`.
- Chat AI and MCP: `cobalt.library.transcript(id, { from, to })` (MCP `library_transcript`, tier free): the same body
  without `status`, and without `words` for the model. The chat's instructions mention it and `said`.
- `GET /capabilities` `features.transcripts`: exactly `about_sound`.

### 47.4 Moments

- `momentsOf`: consecutive lines grouped until a stretch holds 120 characters or 12 s; at most 60 a post.
- The cron, after the search indexer and before the word list (45 s): removes the moments of posts without a `done`
  transcript and past each post's last stretch, then embeds (document mode, 8 a call) every stretch whose text changed
  (`input_hash` = SHA-256 of `m1` + text). The Space asleep: the rest wait for the next run.

### 47.5 Search (`GET /library/search`, the chat's `cobalt.library.search`)

- The whole heard text counts for the text match (34.6's bonus), not just its first 1,200 characters.
- A post's score is the larger of its own vector's and its best moment's (both cosines with the query), plus the bonus. A
  post with no vector but a moment is a hit.
- Each hit may carry `said` (left out when empty): the lines whose text holds the query (lowercase, whole), at most 5, as
  `{ start, end, text, match: "words" }`; else, when its best moment scores at least `max(0.25, own score - 0.05)`, that
  moment as `{ start, end, text, match: "meaning" }`. The chat's search gives `said: [{ at, end, text, match }]`.

### 47.6 Clients (app)

- The detail of a video post, on its own video tab, loads the transcript once (`SubtitleSession`,
  `apple/Cobalt/Screens/Detail/Subtitles.swift`). While it has lines (`done`), the hero's bar gets a CC button; nothing
  for any other state. Subtitles show **under the video** (default; two lines of room, the current word lit) or **on the
  picture** (long press or right click on CC); on/off and the place are remembered for every video. Full screen is the
  system player and shows none.
- "heard" in the about section lists the timed lines with their times; a tap plays the video from there, the line being
  said is lit, and the list copies as text or as a .srt made on the device from the lines.
- Search: a "said in it" strip under the search line shows the hits with `said` (their first moment, its time, "by
  meaning" for `meaning`); a tap opens the post playing from that moment with subtitles on for that opening.

### 47.7 Files, tests, deploy

- Files: `api/src/transcripts.ts`, `api/src/{about-writer,about-probe,embed,chat,chat-tools,app-routes,index}.ts`,
  `api/src/registry/ops/library.ts`, `api/helper/{lib,server}.js`, `d1/migrations/0030_transcripts.sql`.
- Tests: `api/test/transcripts.test.ts`, `api/test/migration-0030.test.ts`, `api/test/about.test.ts`,
  `api/test/about-helper.test.ts`.
- **Deploy**: migration 0030, then the API (Worker and container: the helper's audio length). No secret, no binding change.

## 48. Search names: teaching search a word (addendum, 2026-10-31; owner: "can we have a UX UI to tinker with embeddings so we can create relationships? So like if I say Lia it's talking about pink cat girl?", then "Implement please"; plan `search-names/PLAN.md`, mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/X9kfuT9k4Xzr8HMnfnscda)

EmbeddingGemma 2 has never heard of Lia, so a search for "Lia" (section 21) only finds titles holding those letters. A
**name** is a word the owner teaches search: `Lia` (also called `lia-chan`, `my model`) means "pink-haired cat girl with
cat ears, my vtuber model", with optional **example** posts ("this is Lia") and **not** posts ("this isn't"). A search
that says a name looks for what it means. Names change search only: no post's index input changes, nothing is
re-indexed, and turning a name off or deleting it undoes it at once. A name with no examples is a plain "same as" word
(`capy` means `capybara`).

### 48.1 Storage (migration `0031_search_names.sql`)

- `search_names`: `id` (16 base62), `name` (as typed, 1..40 characters after trimming), `name_key` (normalized: lower
  case, trimmed, inner whitespace one space; unique), `aliases` (JSON array of normalized words, at most 8, each 1..40),
  `meaning` (1..300 characters, trimmed), `strength` (0..1, default 0.5), `cutoff` (0..1, default 0.5), `enabled` (0|1),
  `model`, `dims`, `vector` (the meaning embedded in query mode), `meaning_hash` (SHA-256 hex of the meaning the vector
  was made from), `point` (48.2), `created_at`, `updated_at`. At most 100 names.
- `search_name_posts`: `name_id`, `post_key` (the `GET /library` post key), `kind` `yes` | `no`, `created_at`. Primary
  key `(name_id, post_key)`: one mark per post per name. At most 200 marks per name. Deleting a name deletes its marks.
- Words are unique across names: a name's `name_key` and aliases may not equal another name's `name_key` or alias
  (`409 error.names.taken`). Aliases equal to the name's own key are dropped; duplicates are dropped.
- Until 0031 is applied every route below answers `503 error.api.generic`, search behaves exactly as before, and
  `features.names` is false (checked in `sqlite_master` like 37.1).

### 48.2 The name's point

- `m` = the meaning's vector, `c` = the normalized average of the vectors of its `yes` posts that are indexed, minus
  0.25 times the normalized average of its indexed `no` posts' vectors, normalized again (no indexed `no` posts: just
  the `yes` average). `point` = normalize((1 - strength) * m + strength * c); only `m`: `m`; only `c`: `c`; neither:
  NULL.
- The point is written on every create or update (marks included) and by the 15-minute cron (below). Saving embeds the
  meaning at once when it changed (one Space call, `QUERY_CALL_MS`); if the Space cannot answer the save still succeeds
  with `vector` NULL (`embedded: false` in the answer) and the point from the examples alone.
- Cron, before the search indexer: names whose `vector` is NULL, whose `meaning_hash` differs from the meaning, or whose
  `model`/`dims` differ from the current ones get their meaning embedded (one batch call, at most 20 a run) and their
  point rewritten; every name's point is rewritten too when any of its marked posts' vectors changed since (simplest:
  every run rewrites every enabled name's point; it is a handful of rows).
- A post **carries** a name when it is marked `yes`, or it is not marked `no`, has a vector, and `dot(vector, point) >=
  cutoff`. Hidden posts (37.2) never carry a name in any answer.

### 48.3 Search (`GET /library/search`, the chat and MCP `library.search`, `chat.rank`, stage pools)

- A query **says** a name when the normalized query holds the name's `name_key` or one of its aliases as whole words
  (bounded by start, end or a character that is not a letter or digit). Only enabled names. Longest words first; one
  word in the query counts for one name.
- With at least one name said: the text sent to the Space is the query, a space, then `(` + each name as
  `<name>: <meaning>` joined by `; ` + `)`, cut to 600 characters. The query vector is then mixed:
  `normalize(qv + sum of the said names' points)` (a name with no point adds nothing). The text match (34.6's
  `TEXT_MATCH_BONUS`) still looks for the query as typed; on top, a post that carries a said name gets
  `TEXT_MATCH_BONUS * w`, where `w` = the share of the query's words (34.6 `queryWords`) that belong to said names'
  words (a query that is only "Lia" gives the full bonus). The two bonuses do not add past one `TEXT_MATCH_BONUS`.
- The name-match fallback (Space asleep, `fallback: "text"`): a post that carries a said name counts as holding that
  name's words.
- `names=0` on `GET /library/search` turns names off for that search (the web and app "compare" view). The answer gains
  `names: [{ "id", "name" }]`: the names that were said and used (always present, may be empty). Each post's `why` (34.6)
  starts with the said names it carries.
- The chat and MCP `library.search` and `chat.rank` use names the same way (they share `searchPosts`).

### 48.4 Routes

All take the app's client key or the web Worker's library service credential, CORS for the web app, `no-store`, like
37.4. Errors: `400 error.names.bad` (the input does not fit 48.1), `404 error.names.not_found`, `409 error.names.taken`
(with `word`: the clashing word), `409 error.names.too_many`, `503 error.api.generic` (before 0031).

The `Name` shape: `{ "id", "name", "aliases": [string], "meaning", "strength", "cutoff", "enabled": bool,
"yes": [post_key], "no": [post_key], "posts": int (posts that carry it now), "embedded": bool (the meaning has a vector
for the current model and meaning), "created_at", "updated_at" }`. `yes`/`no` newest mark first.

- `GET /library/names`: `{ "status": "success", "names": [Name] }`, oldest first.
- `POST /library/names`: body `{ "name", "meaning", "aliases"?, "strength"?, "cutoff"?, "enabled"?, "yes"?: [post_key],
  "no"?: [post_key] }` → `201 { "status": "success", "name": Name }`. A post key in both lists: `400`.
- `PATCH /library/names/:id`: any of the create fields (lists replace the marks) plus `"mark": [{ "post_key", "kind":
